# Nullovation City: everything to know

State as of version 0.1.76 (October 2026). The tool file is about 2 MB; since 0.1.67 the hosted page installs as an app, since 0.1.73 cities sync through a private GitHub repository, since 0.1.74 the city works on a phone without its bar or panels running off the screen, since 0.1.75 with a phone's own long press, back and guide, and since 0.1.76 a phone adds a task with + Task, mark keys and its own calendar. 0.1.38, one skill for the city, was built in a chat and reached claude.ai, and the repo at 0.1.72; 0.1.39 was built from 0.1.37.

## 1. What it is

Nullovation City is Abdurrahman's personal project tracker, drawn as an isometric pixel-art city. Every project is one building: click it for a quick status bubble, or step inside for everything else. It is one self-contained HTML file that runs offline in the browser, with everything stored locally. It holds his real projects, around 13 of them.

A building is a company that is always alive, whatever the project's status. Buildings never show progress or stage; the bubbles carry status. Since 0.1.31 a neglected building gathers garbage round it, on the ground, never on the building.

What Abdurrahman keeps:
- The tool: `index.html` at the root of the repo, which GitHub Pages also serves. A chat delivers it as `nullovation-city.html`. Since 0.1.67 the hosted page installs as an app, on the PC and the phone (The app, below).
- His data file: a JSON file on disk that the tool keeps up to date.
- Exported backups.
- The skills: `nullovation-city`, one skill for tasks, plans, updates and building designs, whose source is in the source, and `d-wizard`, which has its own repo.
- The source, in a GitHub repo that Abdurrahman pushes; Claude never pushes. It travels to chats as the archive `nullovation-city-source-v<version>.zip`: code, art scripts, building animations, tests, fonts, the two skills' sources, `CLAUDE.md` for Claude Code, and a copy of this file. Section 8 covers how it moves between chats and Claude Code.

## 2. Features

### The city map

- The map is a grid of plots, 5 to 11 each way, so it can be a rectangle, such as 7 by 5. Edit City, in Settings, adds or takes off a line on any side, or a ring all round (Edit City, below). Each plot is 4 by 4 tiles, with streets between plots.
- Empty plots become parks, in seven kinds: garden, park, plaza, pond, and since 0.1.21 playground, sculpture garden, and mini golf. A park's kind comes from a hash of its place in the city, never the same as a neighbor, so adding a kind reshuffles every park once. Edit City can pick any park's kind.
- Streets vary by segment, fixed so they look the same every visit: repair patches, cracks, manholes, drains, center lines (dashed, double, none, turn arrows), sidewalk paving, crossings (zebra, stop line, none), and crossing centers (plain, manhole, yellow box, small roundabout). Like the parks and the props, they are dealt by their place in the city, so they stay beside their buildings when a line or a ring is added; the ring road round the edge is always the avenue with a double line.
- Zoom steps are 1x, 2x, 3x, 4x, 6x, and 8x. At even steps every building pixel lands on whole screen pixels. Since 0.1.75 a touch screen's number counts screen pixels, in whole numbers (S2), so a phone, with three to a point, reads 2x where it read 0.7x. At 3x a building is first enlarged crisply to 2x, then scaled down smoothly, so it stays sharp.
- Drag to pan. Q and E jump to the previous or next building, Enter opens the one-line add (on the selected building's project when one is selected, since 0.1.56), as + Task in the side bar does (0.1.76), a double-click on a building (a double tap on a phone, since 0.1.74) or F then Enter steps inside it, F opens its shortcut wheel (a long press on a phone, since 0.1.75), and Esc backs out one layer at a time.
- The bar at the bottom right holds Traffic, Bubbles, Time of day, Postcard, Zoom, and a gear that opens Settings. On a small screen its buttons wear their icons only, since 0.1.74 (The city on a phone, below).
- Bubbles and Time of day open drop-ups: stacks of the same buttons as the bar, rising above the one clicked. Each option has its own icon, and the bar's button wears the chosen option's icon and label. The current choice shows pressed, arrow keys and Enter work, and Esc or a click outside closes.
- Time of day:
  - Auto (clock icon) follows the computer's clock, passing through dawn and dusk, at the hours set in Settings, Look: night from 19:00 and day from 06:30 by default.
  - Day (sun).
  - Dusk (a sun on the horizon) holds the halfway light. Dawn looks the same, so it is listed once.
  - Night (moon).
- Bubbles:
  - Bubbles (alert icon) shows red and yellow urgency.
  - Red only (flag).
  - Tasks (task icon) puts a near-white bubble over every building with that project's count of open tasks, 0 included.
  - No bubbles (cross).
- Hovering a building and the city's star (0.1.39, changed at 0.1.40 and 0.1.41): see Tasks on the map, below.
- Ghost tasks creeping round their blocks (0.1.47): see Ghost tasks, below.
- Postcard saves the current view as a PNG, with a caption and the date, at screen size; Settings, Look, can leave out the caption or double the size.

### Street props (0.1.22)

Bus stops, vending machines, holo billboards and bins stand round the blocks, made with PixelLab (section 4, Street props) and drawn at half size like the buildings.
- **Where:** on the sidewalk. On a block's south-east and south-west sides a prop stands near the curb and reaches back over the plot's rim; on its north-east and north-west sides it stands near the plot, behind it as seen from the camera, so a tall building may hide part of it.
- **Facing:** each faces its own street: fronts on the south-east and south-west sides, backs on the north-east and north-west.
  - A front faces one of the two near streets as PixelLab drew it: the bus stop's and billboard's the south-east, the vending machine's and the bin's the south-west. `src/buildings/props.json` records it as `faces`. The back faces the opposite street, the same diagonal turned around.
  - Mirrored, a view turns to the other street on the same side of the camera, so each view is drawn as is on the side it faces and mirrored on the other.
  - Until 0.1.23 the tool took every front as facing the south-west street, which turned the bus stops and billboards sideways on every side.
- **Which prop where:** bus stops by a fixed pattern (a fifth of block sides); vending machines mid-block; holo billboards 0.62 tiles from a corner, just past where a zebra lands; bins on a building's two front sides, where people walk in. At most two to a side, kept apart.
- **Fixed:** the plan comes from the block's place in the city and its side, like the street details, so it looks the same every visit and stays put when the map grows; planting a building only adds or takes away that block's bins.
- **Settings, City:** street props on or off, each kind on or off, How many, and Deal again (Settings, below). Off, a kind only goes; it moves nothing else.
- **People** walk through them, drawn in front or behind by depth. Props show with the traffic off too, and their neon (the billboard's screen, the bus stop's sign, the vending machine's front) keeps glowing by night.
- On the 5 by 5 test city: 51 props on 47 of 100 block sides (20 bus stops, 16 vending machines, 12 billboards, 3 bins).

### Traffic

The city has 12 cars, 2 small buses, and 2 delivery drones on a 5 by 5 map, scaled with the map's area. 20 people roam the whole city, and 2 cyclists ride its sidewalks. Settings, Life, keeps any of them in, and scales the street traffic and the walkers from a quarter to twice those counts.

- **People (0.1.20):** 5 px wide and 6 tall, drawn in code from row strings in `src/js/45-traffic.js` (`BODY`, `personRows`).
  - They face the way they move: toward you the face shows under the hair, walking away the head is all hair, and walking left mirrors the sprite.
  - Looks: about half have long hair (framing the face, and filling the back from behind), and half of those wear a dress over bare legs.
  - At most one item each: a courier's coral pack (6%), a tote bag (12%), a cap whose brim points the way they walk (15%), or a phone (10%). The phone and the pack's screen glow cyan and keep that color by night, so the crowd twinkles after dark.
  - Runners (3%): arms and legs spread, twice as fast, never stopping, with no item or companion.
  - Companions: some walk with a kid, 5 px tall (6%), or a 4 by 3 dog (5%), a step to their right; they skip the benches.
  - Cyclists: 2 on a 5 by 5 map, scaled with the map's area, kept apart from the people count. They ride the sidewalk loops at three times walking speed, cross at zebras (cars wait for them too), and never stop.

- Cars keep to the right, turn at crossings, stop at stop lines, wait at zebras, and keep a gap. They do not yet yield inside crossings, so turning paths can briefly overlap.
- Buses pull up to a bus stop on their side of the street (on their right, along their lane) and pause there for 2 to 3.5 seconds, once as they pass; cars behind them hold back.
- At night, cars and buses driving toward you show two warm headlights and a soft cone of light on the road ahead. Cars driving away show red tail lights.
- Drones hop between busy buildings.
- **Claude's drones (0.1.37):** while Claude Code works on a building (any of its sessions working), six coral drones with a warm yellow light, and no parcel, fly in high from the map's edge, settle to between 24 and 96 px up, and work round it on a loose ring 1.2 to 2.4 tiles from its middle, pausing and changing height. When no session there is working, they fly off the edge and are gone. They follow Traffic, Still, and the city's drones setting (Settings, Life), and are `cdrones` in `45-traffic.js`, checked once a second.
- **A street party (0.1.46):** when a milestone is finished, its block dances, six party drones loop over it and the nearest bus pulls up to watch (Celebrations, below). `parties` and `pdrones` in `45-traffic.js`.
- Busyness comes from a project's activity log over the past 7 days, each event fading out over the week; a task going overdue (0.1.69) is not an event and counts for nothing. A building with busyness of 0.3 or more draws its own crowd.
- The crowd size is a setting in Settings, Life: None, Small, Medium, or Large, for 0, 8, 14, or 20 extra people per busy building. Medium is the default, and the whole city is capped at 150 people: the walkers stay under it, and the crowds take what room is left.
- People walk the sidewalks, cross at zebras, gather in groups and talk, sit on park benches, and go in and out of buildings.
- The Traffic button, and the Traffic switch in Settings, Life, turn all of it on or off. Motion set to Still stops it too.

### Urgency and bubbles

Each project picks an urgency system:
- **None.**
- **Work until finished:** its open tasks count.
- **One task per N days:** a pace. If no task is finished in time, the project turns red.

Due dates also count: tasks due soon or overdue jump to the top of the status bubble.

Settings, Projects, pauses the systems, never the due dates: rest days are weekdays that do not count for either system, and away until a date keeps every system quiet until that day, from which each counts afresh. Red and yellow bubbles float over buildings that need attention, showing a count, or "!" for a single item. Until 0.1.58 the side bar's Urgent section listed red projects; since 0.1.59 a building falling behind its pace is its leader's news (The leaders' news, below), and the count line under search still counts the red and the yellow.

### Garbage (0.1.31)

A city you leave alone fills with litter, and a hover truck clears it once you are back on it. Visuals only: garbage never shows in the status card, the recap or the status brief.
- **The points, one piece of garbage each, round the building that earned them:**
  - One task per N days: yellow (one period missed) 5; red (two periods) 10, and 10 more for each period after: 20 at three, 30 at four.
  - Work until finished: red (no task finished for a day) 10, and 10 more for each day after. Its yellow, a task finished in the last 24 hours, adds nothing.
  - Each overdue task: 5 for each day late, from the day after its due date: 2 days 10, a week 35, a month 150.
  - Due within 2 days but not yet late adds nothing, though its bubble is red.
- **Rest days and away:** rest days pause the urgency points, not the late ones. Away pauses both: no building is yellow or red, so their garbage goes, and a late task keeps what it had on the day you set away, growing again from the day you are back. The city records the days away as `city.awaySpans`.
- **The ceiling:** 1,000 pieces for the whole city since 0.1.32 (500 before), none per building. Over it, every building's share shrinks in proportion, the leftovers going to the largest remainders.
- **Where:** first the building's front strip (lifted onto its plot) and front sidewalks, then its back sidewalks, the gutters of the streets beside it, the nearest park, and the sidewalks across the street, then piling up on its own ground; 1,000 spots a building since 0.1.32 (640 before), so one forgotten building can carry the whole ceiling. 1,000 pieces cost nothing measurable. Litter gathers in clusters, by corners and curbs. Each spot and its kind is dealt by the building's place in the city, so garbage stays put between visits and moves with its building.
- **The pieces:** drawn in code at double detail and shown at half size, like the props: loose paper and wrappers, sticky notes, cans and bottles with a cyan glint (which brightens at night, as the neon does), and dark, white and lying trash bags. Night colors come from the tool's palette, with no glow of their own. Now and then a loose paper hops a few pixels in the wind, never far from its spot.
- **Cleanup:** any drop calls a hover garbage truck, a partial one too.
  - It drives in from the map's edge at one end of the street on the block's south-east or south-west side (dealt by its place), faster the further it comes (3 to 7 tiles a second), and stops by the block.
  - Three of its crew, in coral with yellow caps and brooms, walk the sidewalk to spots of their own round the block and sweep for about 3.4 seconds; the pieces go with a sparkle, nearest first, and those spilled far away go when the crew are back. The truck leaves by the other end.
  - At most 3 trucks out at once; the rest wait their turn. The truck hovers with a shadow under it, bobs a pixel, and shows headlights or tail lights at night.
  - Traffic off: garbage goes with the sparkle alone. Motion on Still: it just goes. What changed while the city was closed shows as it is on load, with no truck. A ticked task, a moved building or a new day shows within a second.
- **Where it shows:** the map, the postcard, and the picture at the top of the project view, which is the map's own drawing of the building in its street. Edit City hides it.
- **The switch:** Settings, Look, Garbage, on by default and kept on this computer. Off, the city looks clean and no truck comes.

### Gus Grimsby, the Garbage Man (0.1.32)

The cleanup crew's chief, grumpy but fair, who tells you what to fix. Designed over three wizard rounds (section 6, The Garbage Man).
- **Where (0.1.59):** his face shows first in the side bar's News section (The leaders' news, below), with a coral ! or his count of tasks, while the city has garbage: on its map, or owed by the rules. He steps away once the last piece is gone and his last word has closed, and goes when the Garbage switch is off. He has no dismiss: what he says comes from the garbage itself. Until 0.1.58 he had his own part of the side bar, between This week and Urgent, with his face, name and title, a [-], his last line and his report.
- **His report, in his bubble (0.1.59):** a click on his face opens his speech bubble, which stays until closed: his last line, then his report, the focus on its first row. Since 0.1.33 he never says which projects are urgent or need attention: the map's bubbles show that.
  - One line for the overdue tasks: "You have 4 overdue tasks. Let's clean them up." ("You have 1 overdue task. Let's clean it up." for one); with no overdue task, no line. Overdue: a late task that makes garbage (past its due date, and not frozen by away). Until 0.1.33 the line led with "2 projects need your urgent attention, 1 project needs your attention."
  - The task to start with, the one that clears the most: "Start with Draw the beds, in Garden planner.", or "Start with any task in Home budget." when that is a project behind its pace. A late task also puts its project back on pace, so it counts both. Titles past 60 characters are cut short.
  - Every building with garbage, the most first: "Garden planner: 2 overdue tasks", "Recipe box: 1 overdue task", and for a building behind its pace with no late task, "Home budget: finish any task", since finishing any of its tasks clears it. A click goes to the building and opens its card. (Until 0.1.33: "Garden planner: urgent, 2 overdue tasks", "Home budget: needs attention".)
- **Minimized (0.1.33 to 0.1.58):** the [-] shrank his part of the side bar to his face with a coral ! on it, kept on this computer. It went with his part of the side bar at 0.1.59; his face in News is that shrunk face now, and the old setting is tidied away.
- **When he speaks up:** in a speech bubble at the map's top left, by the side bar (under the Menu button on a phone, since 0.1.74), with his face, name and a close button, and his report under his line since 0.1.59. It closes with its button, Esc in it, or by itself after 14 seconds; once clicked, it stays. A leader's bubble gives way to him and comes back after. Four moments:
  - The first open of the day with garbage in it, one of three lines picked at random: "You're back. {tasks} between this city and clean streets. Start with {task}.", "Took you long enough. {tasks} waiting. {task} first.", "I cannot clean what you don't fix. {tasks}. Start with {task}." {tasks} is the tasks between the city and clean streets: every late task, plus one for each project behind its pace with no late task of its own; {task} is the task to start with, capitalized when it starts a sentence.
  - The garbage passing 50 pieces: "The city is getting dirty! We should do something about it."
  - Passing 200: "The city is filthy! This is unacceptable!"
  - The last piece going, after the truck: "Clean streets. Don't get used to it.", "Not a wrapper in sight. I'll be off, then." or "Fine. Good work. Don't make me come back."
  - Each alarm speaks once as the total climbs past it, at most once a day, and only the worst one passed speaks; nothing speaks on load but the day's first word. The days are kept in this browser.
  - The bubble is at most 400 px wide (380 until 0.1.57). Squeezed into less than 360 px of map, as beside the project view, it puts its face and close button on top and the words under them, across the bubble (`fitSay` in `00-util.js`, 0.1.57); Proxima's does the same.
- **Never pieces:** his lines and his report count tasks and projects, never pieces of garbage.
- **His face (0.1.48):** his portrait, Abdurrahman's 256 by 256 pixel art (`src/portraits/gus.png`): the yellow cap with the truck's teal badge, grey brows and walrus mustache, coral overalls with a gold band, a broom over his shoulder. It shows shrunk in a dark coral frame, 48 by 48 in News (64 in his part of the side bar until 0.1.58), and 100 by 100 in his speech bubble (80 until 0.1.57), smooth, as the map shrinks its buildings. Until 0.1.47 a faceless stand-in drawn in code, 28 by 22 px at twice the size, took its place.

### Tasks on the map (0.1.39 to 0.1.41)

The first of the task ideas (section 6, Tasks), from Tasks, Round 2, M1 to M6, with Abdurrahman's changes to the star after trying it (0.1.40), and the star's colors and What is next's layout picked from mockups (0.1.41, Star and What is next, Round 1).
- **The hover (M1 to M3):** with the mouse resting on a building for 250 ms, a white bubble with its count of open tasks (0 included) joins its bubbles on the left, so the bubbles already there stay where they are, and its next task shows in one line under them, centred, in the Tasks view's near white, cut short past 40 characters with the full title as its tooltip. A pinned or starred next task carries a small pin or star. In the Tasks view, which already shows the count, only the line is added. It shows whatever Bubbles says, No bubbles included, since you asked for it; not on the selected building (its card is open), in Edit City, while planting or moving, while the shortcut wheel is open, or in the postcard. A press, a right-click or leaving the building takes it away; letting go starts it again. The building's name tip by the pointer stays as it was. The line is `#hoverTask`, placed each time the map is drawn (`placeHoverLabel` in `40-map.js`).
- **Pins (M4, M5):** a project can pin one open task as its next one. The pinned task comes first in What is next, the status card, the hover, the project's status and the summary for Claude (`nextTask` and `bubbleTodos` honor it), even from another milestone; What is next says Pinned beside its label and names the pinned task's own milestone. Pinning another task moves the pin. It clears only when its task is ticked (anywhere: the list, the card, the panel, Tick it, Claude's asked-for tick), deleted, or unpinned; reopening the task does not bring it back.
- **The star (M6):** one open task in the whole city can wear the star: a purple bubble with a gold star over its building (0.1.40 made it cyan on night blue, a misreading of the request; 0.1.41 brought the gold back, A1), beside the urgency (or Tasks) bubble and before Claude's. It shows with Bubbles, Red only and Tasks, and hides with No bubbles and in the postcard. Starring a task pins it in its project too; after that the two are separate, so unstarring keeps the pin and pinning another task keeps the star. Starring another task moves the star, with the toast "The star moved to <task>." Ticking, deleting or unstarring takes it off.
- **Where to pin and star (M4, changed at 0.1.40):** the pin is a small icon button, hollow until pressed, on every open row of the status card and of the project view (shown while the row is pointed at or has the focus, and always when pressed), and at What is next's top right, where a pressed pin says Pinned (0.1.41). The star is set only in the task panel, as Star for the city / Starred: Abdurrahman found a star on every row too much for a feature used rarely. The task panel also has Pin as next / Pinned. A starred task shows a gold Starred mark, not a button, at What is next's top right (0.1.41; a cyan star mark beside the task at 0.1.40). After a click in What is next the focus stays on its pin, even when the next task changes. A click on a pin keeps the focus on it and neither opens nor drags the task. A done task has none.
- **The starred task in the side bar (0.1.40):** while a task wears the star, a card under search, above This week, shows its building, Starred with the star, the task (two lines at most) and its project, purple with a gold edge (0.1.41; cyan at 0.1.40). A click opens the task in its project; ticking, deleting or unstarring takes the card away. `renderStar` in `70-menu.js`.
- **What is next (0.1.40, 0.1.41):** a widget of its own, a shade lighter than the project view (#2E2748) with an edge round it (0.1.40). Since 0.1.41 it is layout B (A3): the label What is next with the pin, and Starred while the task wears the star, at its top right; the task's own milestone with the due date as a chip at the right of its line ("Due Fri 23 Oct", "Due tomorrow", "2 days overdue", red when late), and nothing without a date; the task in 17 px type, red with a ! when overdue; Open in Claude on its row (Copy task beside it until 0.1.62; Copy is in its panel since 0.1.63); and the other overdue tasks' note under them. While its task wears the star, the box is purple (#3A2766) with a gold edge (A2).
- **Not work:** pinning and starring change neither Updated nor urgency, and write nothing in Activity. Esc in the task panel, which undoes the task's edits, leaves them alone, since they live on the project.
- **Data:** a project's `pin` and `star` hold a task id or are empty (section 3). `tidyMarks` clears any that point at a task no longer open before every save, and reading a city keeps the first star only.

### Recurring tasks and one-line adding (0.1.42)

The second of the task ideas (section 6, Tasks), from Tasks, Round 2, R1 to R4, O1 and O2, with R1 reasoned out at the build from Abdurrahman's own words. `22-repeat.js` holds the model and the reading of a typed line; `66-quickadd.js` the quick add.
- **How a repeat comes back (R1):** ticking a repeating task moves the same task on to its next date (R4) and it waits: out of the status card, What is next, the hover count, the bubbles, urgency, Due this week, Gus and the garbage, until that day, when it is back and due that day. In the project view it keeps its place in its milestone, dimmed, with its repeat sign for the square and "Back Thu 22 Oct", so it can be opened, changed or stopped; its panel says "Ticked. Back Thu 22 Oct, due that day." A nearer due date brings it back sooner. A new repeat shows at once, due on its first date. A repeat not ticked by its date stays one overdue task, garbage and all, until it is ticked; nothing piles up.
- **Undo:** a tick in the list, the card or Tick it shows a toast, "Water the beds comes back Fri 23 Oct.", with Undo; unticking in the card while the line is still struck through puts it back too. In the task panel, Esc undoes it, as it undoes every edit there; the Done line is written when the panel closes, as for any task.
- **Schedules (R2):** every day, every N days (2 to 365), weekly on chosen weekdays (the buttons run Saturday to Friday, like rest days; one stays pressed at least), monthly on a date (1st to 31st; a month without it uses its last day, and the panel says so), yearly (a day and a month; 29 February uses the 28th in other years). The task panel's Repeat row, under the due date, sets them; a task with no due date gets the schedule's first date from today; No repeat stops it, bringing back a waiting one at once. Repeats show a small repeat sign beside the date in the project view's rows and the status card.
- **The next date (R3), `nextRepeatDue`:** weekdays, monthly dates and years keep their rhythm: the first such date after the later of today and the due date, so a late tick skips what was missed and an early one does not come back the same week. Every day and every N days count from the day of the tick.
- **One task moves on (R4):** the task keeps its id, title, milestone and description; only Activity's Done line records each tick, and it counts as work for urgency. The week's recap reads Done lines, so a repeat ticked counts there, once per week by its title. Pins and the star clear on a repeat's tick, as on any tick (M5). A milestone with a repeat in it never counts as finished.
- **The one-line add (O1):** marks typed in a task's line are taken out of its title. !fri (or !friday), !today, !tomorrow, !20, !oct 20 (or !20 oct, or !2026-10-20) give the due date, the next such date that is not past. #daily, #weekly, #monthly, #yearly, #every day and #every 3 days give a repeat: #weekly on the due date's weekday, or on each weekday marked (!mon !thu #weekly); #monthly and #yearly on the due date's day; with no date a repeat starts today. @name puts it in the milestone whose name starts with it (case, Arabic letter forms, and hyphens for spaces aside), or a new milestone by that name when none fits. A word that does not read as a mark stays in the title, so "#1 priority" and "bob@mail.com" are left alone. Marks are English; the title can be in any language. Since 0.1.53, +create, +think or +errand gives the task's shift (All tasks and shifts, below). Under the field, a line says what was understood before Enter ("In Launch · Due Fri 23 Oct · Repeats weekly on Fri"), or what is missing ("Type the task too", "/nope fits no project"); empty and focused, it names the marks. ^ and * (pin and star) and #repeat alone were left out (O1).
- **Where (O2):** the project view's add field under each milestone's +, which puts the task in that milestone unless @ says otherwise; a new add field at the foot of the status card's list, "Add a task", which added to the current milestone (else the last, else a new To do), cleared, and stayed focused for the next one, until 0.1.76, when it went (The City on a Phone, Round 2, A3: "you don't add tasks from here"); and the quick add: T, anywhere outside a field and when no dialog is open, opens one line over the map, "A task, with /project !date #repeat @milestone". /name picks the project the same way @ picks a milestone; without it the task goes to the project open in the view, else the selected building, and with neither it asks for one. Enter adds it and closes, with a toast "Added to Garden planner: Weed the beds." and Open; Esc or a click elsewhere closes it. The controls guide names T. Rethought at 0.1.56 (The line over the map, rethought, below).
- **Data:** a task may carry `repeat` (`{ every: 'day', n }`, `{ every: 'week', days }`, `{ every: 'month', day }` or `{ every: 'year', month, day }`) and `waiting: true` while a ticked repeat waits for its due date. Reading a city keeps a whole repeat only, and a wait only while its date is ahead; `tidyMarks` clears a wait whose day has come before every save, so a later due date cannot hide it again. A project export loaded back as a plan keeps its repeats, and a tasks file or Claude's add-tasks may carry one in the same shape.

### The line over the map, rethought (0.1.56)

Abdurrahman's task "Rethink 1 line task add" in Nullovation City Hall, UX Mastery, designed in Rethink the One-Line Add, Round 1, with the leader's toast asked in the chat (section 6, The one-line add, rethought). `66-quickadd.js` holds it; the reader's `spans` live in `22-repeat.js`, the map's Enter and double-click in `40-map.js`, the toast in `00-util.js`.
- **Opening it (E1):** Enter on the map, with no field, button, link or row focused, opens the line, on the selected building's project when one is selected; T still opens it anywhere outside a field. Since 0.1.76 + Task, right after + New project in the side bar, opens it too, on the PC and, through Menu, on a phone (The city on a phone, below). With the wheel open, Enter still steps inside its building.
- **Stepping inside (E1, his note):** a double-click on a building, by the browser's own count, so two single clicks never do; the first click's building counts, since the map may glide under the pointer between the two. Since 0.1.74 a double tap on a phone does it too (The city on a phone, below). Not on a park, in Edit City, or while planting or moving. F then Enter, through the wheel, and the Enter buttons on the status card and the wheel do it too.
- **The last project (I1):** with no project open in the view or selected, the line starts with /<the project last added to> filled in, to keep, change or delete: the project with the newest "Added todo" line in Activity, so an add from any field counts. With none on record it starts empty. A line holding only its project shows the project under the field, without "Type the task too".
- **Suggestions (P1, P2, I1):** a mark at the start of a word opens a list under the line: / the projects, @ the line's milestones, ! dates, # repeats, + shifts. Up and Down move, Tab or a click picks (Tab takes the first when nothing is highlighted), and Esc closes the list before the line. A pick writes the whole name over the mark, hyphens for spaces, with one space after it, the caret after it. Enter adds the task with the highlighted pick (P1); with nothing typed after the mark nothing is highlighted, so Enter adds the line as typed.
  - **/**: every project, with its picture and its open tasks. Alone, the projects added to most recently first, then the rest by name (I1); typed, the names that are it, then those that start with it, then those with a word that does, then those whose letters come in that order (/gp finds Garden planner, I1). The highlighted project lights its building.
  - **@ (P2):** the milestones of the line's project, the one a task lands in without @ first, then the project's order, those in the archive last, dimmed with the archive's icon; then "New milestone" with what was typed, when no name is it. Typed, the same order of fit as /. With no project yet: "Pick a project with / first", which nothing picks.
  - **!**: Today, Tomorrow and the next five days by name, each with its date ("Fri 30 Oct"); a weekday's name finds today or tomorrow too. A number or date typed, such as !25, is offered first with its date.
  - **#**: Daily, Weekly, Monthly and Yearly, each saying when it starts from the line's own date ("On Fri, starts Fri 30 Oct"), and Every N days, which writes #every and leaves the number to type.
  - **+**: Create, Think and Errand in their tags, with their hints.
- **Only here (W1):** the project view's add field reads the same marks as before, with no list and no colors, as the status card's did until it went at 0.1.76.
- **On a phone (0.1.76):** four mark keys under the line, Project, Milestone, Date and Repeat, and Pick a date at the end of the date list, the phone's own calendar (The city on a phone, below).
- **Colors (I1):** no chips (his note: "Just make the special commands have unique colors"): each kind of mark shows in its own color as typed: /project pink (#FF8FBF), @milestone sky blue (#8EC8FF), !date gold (#FFD27A), #repeat peach (#FFAE85), and a +shift in its shift's color. The field's own text is see-through under a colored copy in the same font, which scrolls with it. A word shows as a mark only once it reads as one, so #1 priority stays plain.
- **The shift tags (S1):** Shift, then Create, Think and Errand under the line, the one the line has pressed. A click picks one, writing or changing its +mark, and a click on the pressed one takes it off; the focus stays in the line.
- **The building lights (I1):** while the line has a project, its building wears the hover ring on the map (`MapView.light`); it goes out when the line closes.
- **Shift+Enter and Ctrl+Enter (I1):** Shift+Enter adds the task and keeps the line open and empty on the same project, its /mark written again when it had one. Ctrl+Enter adds it and opens it in its panel, ready for a description. The line's head names them: "Enter adds · Shift+Enter adds and stays · Ctrl+Enter adds and opens".
- **The toast (I1, and the chat):** the building's leader says it: the leader's face at 40 px (the silhouette without a portrait), the name in its color over the building's, then "Task added: <task>.", with Open and Undo. A building with no leader keeps "Added to <project>: <task>.", with Undo too. Undo takes the task back out with its "Added todo" line, and a milestone the task made with its line; puts Updated back; closes the task's panel when Ctrl+Enter opened it, writing nothing for it; and reopens the line with what was typed, to edit and add again. With the line open and something new typed in it, Undo leaves the line alone.
- **Data:** nothing new in the city or the browser.

### Reports (0.1.43 to 0.1.45)

From the fourth of the task ideas (section 6, Tasks), Tasks, Round 2, P1 to P3, built before the third at Abdurrahman's call on 19 October. `74-reports.js` holds it. The weekly review (V2 to V4) was built at 0.1.43 as a third tab and taken out at 0.1.44, after he tried it (section 6, Tasks); the productivity inspector (V1), Proxima Pace, lives at the top of Reports since 0.1.45 (below).
- **The panel (P1):** a Reports button sits beside Settings at the foot of the side bar; it, or R anywhere outside a field with no dialog open, turns the side bar into Reports, as Settings does, with the map in view and at its size. The back arrow, Esc or R again bring the lists back, with the focus on Reports. Reports and Settings share the side bar, so opening one closes the other. On a narrow window R opens the drawer on Reports. Two tabs with their names, Weeks and Records, and the arrow keys between them; the tab and Week or Month are kept in this browser, and it opens again on the tab last used (Weeks, for a browser last left on 0.1.43's Review).
- **What counts:** every count is of ticks: Activity's Done lines, less those a Reopened line took back (`taskTicks`), so a repeat counts each time it is ticked, unlike the recap's once a week. A done task whose Done line the log no longer keeps (it holds 400 lines a project) counts on the day it was finished. Claude Code's finish lines, added and removed tasks never count.
- **Weeks (P2):** "Tasks finished each week": a bar for each of the last 12 Saturday-to-Friday weeks (`weeklyTicks`), with its count over it, this week's last and hatched as so far, and three dates under them; over the bars, this week so far, the 12 weeks' total and the average a week. "By project": Week or Month, arrows back to the first week or month with a tick and on to now, and each building's tasks finished in it, the most first, with its picture and a teal bar (`ticksByProject`). It opens on this week; a bar picks its week, and that bar turns gold. A row shows the building on the map.
- **Records (P2, P3):** the streak (`streakOf`): days in a row with a task finished, with a flame, then what today does ("Finish a task today to make it 7 days.", "Today counts already.", or a day off that cannot break it) and the longest with its first and last day. Rest days and days away never break it (P3): one with no tick is passed over, one with a tick counts, and today does not break it before its first tick. The busiest building of the month (`busiestByMonth`): its picture and name in gold, and how many it finished; buildings tied share it; then each earlier month with a tick, at most 11. Milestones finished (`milestonesFinished`): every task in them done, dated by the last one, newest first, with the project; 8 show, then Show all; a click opens the project. Overdue tasks over time is for later; by weekday and best weeks were left out.
- **Kept up:** a tick anywhere redraws the open tab at once.
- **Data:** nothing new. Reports keep their tab and Week or Month in this browser (`nullovation-city:reports`).

### Proxima Pace, Productivity Inspector (0.1.45)

V1 of Tasks, Round 2, designed over two wizard rounds (section 6, The Productivity Inspector). A co-pilot who reads the city's instruments and calls out the numbers. `76-proxima.js` holds her.
- **Where (J1):** at the top of Reports, above the tabs: her face, name and title, her line (with a sky-blue bar beside it), and her three reports as tiles, Daily, Weekly and Monthly, each with the day, week or month it is on. A tile not read yet has a sky-blue dot and a sky-blue name, and the Reports button wears the same dot ("Reports (R). Proxima has a report for you."). Her line is the biggest report not read yet's first line, else the daily one's. A click opens the report. With nothing to report yet (a new city): "Her first report comes tomorrow."
- **Her reports (J3):** the daily report, on yesterday, waits in Reports and never speaks; the weekly report, on the last Saturday-to-Friday week, and the monthly one, on the last month, she brings herself on the first open after their period ends (or within a minute, while the city stays open), each once, in a speech bubble at the map's top left with her face, name, line, Read and a close button. Read opens Reports and the report. The bubble closes by itself after 14 seconds; closed unread, the report keeps its dot. With both due she brings the weekly one, then the monthly one. A report on a period that ended before the city's first record is not given.
- **Never with Gus:** she waits while Gus speaks, and when he speaks while she does, her bubble goes and she says it again after him. She waits while a report or another panel is open.
- **A report, in a panel:** her face and the report's first line, the period, then (L1):
  - in every report: Tasks finished, against the period before ("7, 4 more than the week before."); Streak, now, and how short of the longest; Milestones finished in the period, with their dates; and what the coming day, week or month holds (Today, This week, This month), from today on, with each task's date, a repeat coming back in it included.
  - weekly and monthly add: By building, the most first; Busiest building, ties shared; Nothing finished, the buildings with open tasks and no tick in the period; Overdue, with the oldest-due task to close first.
  - monthly adds: Repeats kept up, each repeat with how many times it was ticked; Notes written, with their dates.
  - From Proxima, her facts (L2): a milestone one task from done (every other task in it done, no repeat, at least two tasks), in every report; next week with nothing due, in the weekly; a repeat (weekly or more often) ticked 3 or more weeks running, in the weekly and monthly.
  - A task opens in its project, a building shows on the map, a milestone opens its project.
- **Every count is of ticks,** as in Reports (`taskTicks`), and the streak is Reports' (`streakOf`).
- **Her lines (V2), word for word:** weekly, one of "Weekly report inbound! {n} tasks landed this week." and "Copy that, week logged: {n} tasks done. Report's ready.", or with no ticks "Quiet week: no tasks landed. Next week holds {due} due."; monthly, one of "Monthly report on deck! {n} tasks in {month}." and "{month} is logged: {n} tasks, {m} milestones. Report's ready.", the second always when nothing landed; daily: "Yesterday: {n} tasks landed. Today's flight plan: {due} due."; the facts: "{milestone} is one task from done: {task}. Let's land it!", "Next week's flight plan is empty. Nothing due yet.", "{repeat}: ticked {k} weeks running. Steady hands!". A count of one reads in the singular. The same report always gets the same line (picked by `hashStr` of its kind and period).
- **Her face (0.1.48):** her portrait, Abdurrahman's 256 by 256 pixel art (`src/portraits/proxima.png`): black hair in a high ponytail with a sky-blue tie, a white earpiece with a side mic, a sky-blue pilot shirt with white epaulettes, and a stopwatch on a lanyard (Sky crew, N2). It shows shrunk in a dark sky-blue frame, 64 by 64 on her card and in a report, and 100 by 100 in her speech bubble (80 until 0.1.57), smooth, like Gus's. Until 0.1.47 a faceless stand-in drawn in code, 28 by 22 px at twice the size, took its place.
- **A finished milestone (0.1.46):** she announces its street party in her speech bubble, with Go to it in place of Read (Celebrations, below).
- **Data:** nothing new in the city. What was read and what she said are kept in this browser (`nullovation-city:proxima`).

### Celebrations (0.1.46)

The sixth of the task ideas (section 6, Tasks), designed in Celebrations, Rounds 1 and 2 (section 6, Celebrations). `49-celebrate.js` holds the particles, the bursts, the party and its lights; `45-traffic.js` the party's crowd, drones and bus; `76-proxima.js` her announcement.
- **A tick bursts (T1):** every tick sets off small particles round its building, never a firework: a burst like the decision wizard's when you type wizard there (26 gold stars that fly out, fall and fade over 1.5 seconds). 16 particles fly out from the building's middle (0.42 of the way from its top to its base), 24 to 50 map px with a slight lift, drift down 14 px, and twinkle out as a dot of their own color over the last fifth of 1.5 seconds. They are drawn in code at the map's scale, 3 to 7 px plus the city's 1 px dark outline, about the size of the people, in the city's colors, which they keep at night like the neon. A tick anywhere counts, through `tickTask`: the status card, the project view, the task panel, Tick it, and Claude's Apply; a repeat's tick too. Unticking plays nothing. They show over everything but the bubbles, on the map and in the project view's picture, which is the map's own drawing.
- **The kinds (T2, T3):** stars, binary 0 and 1 (cyan), gold 0 and 1, dollar signs, lightning, wizard sparkles, hearts, leaves, game buttons (four colors and a d-pad), speech bubbles, coins (which turn as they fly) and confetti (bits in seven colors). Every building starts on stars. `PARTICLE_KINDS` in `20-model.js`.
- **Picking:** the project view's Building panel, opened from the building's picture, has Particles above the generic buildings: a tile per kind, at rest with three of them, playing its burst while pointed at or focused. A click picks it, presses it, and plays it on the map. Picking is not work: Updated stays and nothing goes in Activity.
- **The street party (S1, S2):** ticking the last open task of a milestone of 2 tasks or more throws a 30 second party round its building.
  - Its particles burst every 2 seconds or so.
  - Party lights: strings across its four streets, three to a street, from sidewalk to sidewalk on two poles 11 px tall with a 3 px sag, a bulb on every other pixel in the city's colors (neon pink, butter, neon cyan, lavender, leaf), chasing along; they keep their colors at night. They are sorted by depth with the figures, so a building in front hides the ones behind it.
  - With the traffic on: those on its block dance (both arms up, one arm out, up, the other, with a hop on each up, turning now and then), and others walk over to dance, across its zebras or out of its front, up to about 20 in all, under the city's 150. Anyone walking on to the block joins in.
  - Six party drones in the colors of its particles fly in high from the map's edge, settle, and loop round it at 34 to 79 px up, rising and dipping.
  - The nearest bus drives over at 1.6 tiles a second by the shortest way to a street with the block on its right, skips its bus stops, and waits at the block's curb, mid-block, cars holding back behind it.
  - When it ends, the lights come down, the dancers walk on, the guests walk off across the first zebra (or into the building), the drones fly off the map's edge, and the bus drives on a second later.
  - With the traffic off, the party has its particles and lights only. Motion on Still shows no particles and no party.
- **Proxima announces it:** in her speech bubble, after anything already waiting, in one of two lines approved word for word, picked by the milestone: "Milestone landed! {milestone}: {n} tasks done in {project}. Street party's on!" and "Copy that, {milestone} is logged: {n} for {n} in {project}. The block's dancing!". Go to it, in place of Read, glides the map to the building, into the open part beside the project view when it is open. She waits for Gus and for an open panel, as for her reports; several milestones are said one after another; while the project view is open her bubble sits beside it. She announces it on Still too.
- **Once a day:** a milestone throws its party once a day, so unticking and ticking again throws no second one; kept in this browser (`nullovation-city:celebrate`). A milestone with a repeat in it never finishes.
- **Data:** a project's `particles`, one of `PARTICLE_IDS`; a city without it, or with a kind the tool does not have, reads as stars.

### Ghost tasks (0.1.47)

The fifth of the task ideas (section 6, Tasks), first named Tasks as cars, designed in Ghost Tasks, Round 1 (section 6, Ghost tasks). `46-ghosts.js` holds it.
- **Which tasks haunt (C1, G1, G2):** every overdue task, and the next task of each building behind its pace: one or more periods behind under One task per N days, or a day without a tick under Work until finished. Those are the buildings that make garbage through urgency (`Garbage.breakdown(p).sys`), and overdue is Gus's (a late task that makes garbage), so the ghosts, the garbage and Gus's count agree. The next task is the one the status card puts first (`bubbleTodos`): the pinned one when there is one, else the first due soon, else the current milestone's; a task both overdue and next is one ghost, so a pinned next task that is not overdue gets a ghost of its own beside the overdue ones. At most 5 to a block, the most overdue first, then the next task; the rest come out as ghosts are ticked. Rest days and away pause the pace, never the due dates, so on them only overdue tasks haunt; a repeat waiting for its next date never haunts. `hauntOf`.
- **The ghost (L1, L1a, L2, L3, C2):** a sheet ghost drawn in code, 9 px wide and 12 tall at 1x (`SHAPE`, `HEMS`): a round head over a hem that ripples through three frames, see-through white inside its edge (`rgba(250, 244, 255, 0.62)`), and two glowing eyes 1 by 2 px. Its edge and eyes glow in its building's bubble color: red (edge #FF3D7F, eyes #E8195A) or yellow (edge #FFC81E, eyes #D98A00), and keep it at night like the neon. By day a faint dark ring round it keeps it clear on the pale sidewalk; at night that ring becomes a soft glow in its edge color. Its eyes look the way it goes: to the side it heads on screen, low coming toward you, high going away.
- **Round its block (C3, M1):** on the sidewalk ring, among the people, who walk through it; it is sorted by depth with them, so a building in front hides it. It creeps at 0.11 to 0.15 tiles a second, about half walking speed, floating 1 to 3 px above a faint shadow and bobbing. It pauses now and then (and sometimes turns back), lingers by its building's front staring in, and at a corner stops, leans round on to the new side with its eyes on it, and carries on (X1, peeks round corners). It never crosses a street. Two meeting head on: one turns back after 2 seconds.
- **Coming and going (M2):** a ghost whose task starts to haunt comes out of its building's front, fading in; when the city opens, or the ghosts show again, they are already on their rounds. A tick anywhere (through `tickTask`: the status card, the project view, the task panel, its own card, Tick it, Claude's Apply) lays it to rest at once: it rises 6 px and fades out where it is, in a second. One whose task stops haunting without a tick (a new due date, the building back on pace) leaves the same way, within a second. Its building moved, it goes round the new block.
- **The hover (X1):** resting the mouse on a ghost shows its task's title by the pointer, as a building's name does; no building is hovered.
- **Its card (C4, X1):** a click opens a small card over the ghost, with a strip in its glow: its project, the task, its due chip ("6 days overdue" in red, or the next task's date, nothing without one), Tick and Enter. The status card and the shortcut wheel close for it, and the ghost holds still while it is open. Tick ticks the task off, with the toast "Ticked off: <task>." and Undo, or moves a repeat on with its own toast. Enter steps inside the building with the task open beside it (`FullView.openTask`). Esc, its close button, a click elsewhere on the map, or a building's status card closes it. Open in Claude on the card was left out.
- **When they show (S1, S2):** Settings, Look, Ghosts, on by default and kept on this computer (`nullovation-city:ghosts`), with its dot and its tab's Reset to defaults. Traffic and No bubbles leave them. Hidden on Motion Still and in Edit City; shown on the postcard and in the project view's picture, the map's own drawing.
- **Data:** nothing new in the city.

### The archive (0.1.49)

The seventh of the task ideas (section 6, Tasks), from Tasks, Round 2, A1 to A3, designed in Archiving, Round 1 (section 6, Archiving). `24-archive.js` holds the model; the Archive view and the buttons live in `60-fullview.js`.
- **By itself (A2, A2a):** a done task goes to its project's archive 7 days after the day of its tick: ticked Mon 12 Oct, it goes on Mon 19 Oct. The city checks on opening and when the day changes (`archiveDueTasks`). On the first open with archiving, every done task ticked a week or more ago went at once (D1). A done task with no tick on record counts its 7 days from the day the city first sees it (`archiveFrom`). Only done tasks archive, so a repeat, which moves on when ticked, never does. Unticking a task before its 7 days keeps it in its list.
- **By hand (A1):** an archive button on each done row in the project view, shown while the row is pointed at or has the focus, like the pin on open rows; Archive beside Done in a done task's panel, which closes the panel; and Archive done tasks in the milestone view, beside Delete milestone, for every done task in it. Each shows a toast, "Archived Draw the beds." or "Archived 6 done tasks from Launch.", with Undo. Archive all done, per project, was left out.
- **A milestone (B1):** one whose every task is archived goes to the archive with them, out of the project view, and comes back as soon as one of them is restored, or a task is added to it with @name (or Claude's add-tasks names it). With every milestone in the archive, the list says "Every milestone is in the archive. Add one to plan what is next."; a task added without @ goes to a new To do. Deleting the last task of a milestone in the archive takes the milestone with it.
- **The Archive (A1, C3):** first in the project's more menu, "Archive (12)", or "Archive" with nothing in it (E1). It opens beside the project view, as a milestone does: a line on what it is, then archived tasks by milestone, the milestone with the newest task first, its tasks newest first, by the day each was ticked. A milestone that went to the archive with its tasks says "Milestone archived". Each task shows the day it was ticked ("Ticked Sat 10 Oct") and Restore. Empty: "Nothing in the archive yet."
- **Folding (0.1.50):** each milestone in the Archive view has a fold toggle before its flag, as in the list. All start open; a click, or Enter on the toggle, folds it to its name and count, and the focus stays on the toggle. The fold is the archive's own, apart from the list's, kept on the milestone as `archiveCollapsed` and travelling with the city; the group with no milestone keeps its fold while the page is open. Opening the archive at a milestone (its foot's count) or at a task (Esc, Back to the archive) unfolds it. Not work.
- **An archived task (C2):** a click opens it in the same task panel as any task, to read: its milestone, the title struck through, "In the archive. Ticked Sat 10 Oct.", Restore and Back to the archive, its description, Open in Claude (with Copy task until 0.1.62), and Delete task, which goes back to the archive. Esc goes back to the archive too, with the task in view.
- **Restore (C1):** brings a task back done, in its old place in its milestone, with a toast, "Restored Draw the beds to First release.", and Undo; it archives itself again 7 days after the restore. A task whose milestone is gone goes where a new task would. Dragging tasks, or moving them with Alt and an arrow, keeps every archived task's place among them (`reorderShown`).
- **Signs of it (E1):** at a milestone's foot, the count of its archived tasks with the archive's icon and no words ("4 in the archive" as its tooltip); a click opens the Archive at that milestone. The milestone view lists its archived tasks dimmed, with the icon, under the rest.
- **What still counts them (B2, A3):** a milestone's count, the project's progress and "todos done", the copies' progress lines, search, Reports (Weeks, the streak, Milestones finished), the recap, Proxima and the builder card. **What leaves them out (A3):** the project view's lists, a milestone's Copy for Claude (its done list) and the city summary (its done lists, and a milestone in the archive). Copy task on an archived task says "State: done, in the archive".
- **Plans and Claude:** a plan loaded over a project (Load plan or tasks, or Claude's replace-plan) leaves its archive alone, each archived task under its own milestone, which stays in the archive with it; a project export loaded as a plan leaves its own archive out. Claude's edit-description never touches an archived task; done finds only open ones.
- **Not work:** archiving and restoring change neither Updated nor urgency, and write nothing in Activity.
- **Data:** a done task may carry `archived: true` with `archivedAt` (milliseconds), and `archiveFrom` (YYYY-MM-DD), the day its 7 days count from after a restore; unticking clears it. Reading a city keeps the archive on done tasks only. A milestone may carry `archiveCollapsed: true` (0.1.50).

### Leaders (0.1.51)

The first part of the leaders designed on 5 October (section 6, Leaders): picking a leader for each building, from Abdurrahman's answers of 20 October (L1 to L3). How they talk and come alive is the leader UX, built from 0.1.57 to 0.1.62 (below, and section 6, Leader UX). `23-leaders.js` holds the model, `63-leaders.js` the side bar, the panel, the picker and the faces.
- **What a leader holds (L2):** a name, a role, a signature color and a portrait. One leader per building, and one leader can lead several. The rest of the October design (look, personality, greeting, bio, voice, lines) waits.
- **The Leaders panel (0.1.61, Leader UX C1 and L3):** a Leaders button, with its own pixel icon (`leader`), at the side bar's foot beside All tasks, over Reports and Settings, turns the side bar into every leader, as Reports and Settings do; the three share the side bar, so opening one closes the others (`Menu.listsBack` brings the lists back once none is open; All tasks shared it from 0.1.61 to 0.1.63, and is on the map since 0.1.64). New leader sits at its top right. Each leader is a row: its face, 48 px, its name in its color, its role, and the buildings it leads by name in order ("No building yet" with none); a click opens it to edit, and the row's tooltip says how many it leads. With none: "No leaders yet. Add one with a portrait, then give it a building." The back arrow or Esc closes it, the focus back on its button. On a narrow window it opens in the drawer. Until 0.1.60 a Leaders section after Due this week held a tile per leader, its face and name.
- **The leader editor:** since 0.1.64 (SideBar Cleanup) at level 1, a panel docked on the map's left like the project view (`#ldView`), under "New leader" or "Leader: <name>" with a close button, while the Leaders list stays in the side bar with the leader's row marked (`aria-current`); in the Leaders panel in the list's place from 0.1.61 to 0.1.63, and a dialog over the city until then. The face and the portrait's buttons sit beside the name, role and color, and Leads under them, its buildings in columns; one column on a narrow panel. It opens over the project view (from the picker) and over All tasks, which are back as they were once it closes; opening the project view or All tasks closes it, its changes already kept, a new leader's draft dropped. Done, Add leader, Cancel, Delete leader, its close button and Esc go back to the list, the focus on the leader, and Esc again closes the panel; the side bar's back arrow closes the panel and the editor with it. On a narrow window it covers the map, the drawer closing, and the drawer comes back on the list after. The face at 128 px; Upload portrait, then Replace portrait and Remove; Name; Role; Signature color, twelve swatches (`LEADER_COLORS`, the city's own colors) and a color picker for any other; and Leads, every building with a checkbox, saying "led by <name>" beside a building another leads.
  - A portrait is a PNG, GIF, JPEG or WebP, at most 1024 px and 3 MB, each refused with a line saying why; 256 by 256 pixel art on a see-through background fits best. It shows shrunk smooth, like Gus's and Proxima's.
  - **Framed by its figure (0.1.52):** every face shows the portrait framed by what is drawn: a square round its pixels (alpha 8 and up, read from the first frame), the foot on the frame's foot and the figure 97% of its height (`HEAD_ROOM`), centred. So a portrait drawn small on a big see-through canvas fills its face like the rest, and one that fills its canvas, like Gus's, stays as it was. A picture with no see-through pixels is cropped square, from the top when tall. The file is never changed, and an animated GIF keeps playing. A face larger than the portrait's own pixels grows in whole pixels. `frameOf`, `measure` and `placed` in `63-leaders.js`; frames are kept by portrait while the page is open.
  - New leader: nothing is kept until Add leader, or Enter in the name or the role; a name is needed ("Give the leader a name."). The toast: "Added Gus Grimsby, who leads Garden planner."
  - Editing: every change applies at once, with Done to close; an emptied name reads as Leader. Delete leader asks first, saying the buildings keep running with no leader and the portrait leaves this browser, then "Deleted <name>."
- **The silhouette:** a leader without a portrait shows a pixel head and shoulders in a darker shade of its signature color, on that color, as the October design said.
- **Picking (L1):** Pick a leader, second in the project's more menu ("Leader: <name>" once there is one), or the leader card, opens a picker beside the project view: No leader ("The building runs on its own"), then each leader with its role and where else it leads ("also leads 1 building"), the one in charge pressed; New leader, which adds one already ticked for this building, and Edit <name>, both opening the editor, over the project view since 0.1.64 (in the side bar's Leaders panel from 0.1.61). A click picks it: "<name> leads Garden planner now." or "Garden planner has no leader now."
- **Where the face shows (L1):**
  - the leader card in the project view, under the building's picture and beside the About: the face at 96 px (64 until 0.1.57), the name in its color, the role, what it says (its greeting, since 0.1.58), and Change; with no leader, "No leader yet, Pick a leader", which opens the picker;
  - the status card, a line under the building's name: the face at 48 px (32 until 0.1.57), the name and the role, and since 0.1.59 what it says there (The leaders' news, below). (The October design left the status bubble out; Abdurrahman picked it on 20 October.)
  - the shortcut wheel's middle, kept for it since 0.1.26: the face, round, 64 px, on the signature color; the wheel's label adds "led by <name>". A click there still reaches the building. A building with no leader keeps the empty frame.
  - the toast when the line over the map adds a task (0.1.56): the face at 40 px, the name and the building, and "Task added: <task>." (The line over the map, rethought, above).
- **Claude knows the leader (L3):** Copy task, a note's and a milestone's copies, and the project's status (so the status brief too) carry "Leader: <name>, <role>" after the project; the city summary has `leaders` and each project's `leader` (Claude reads and updates the city, below).
- **Not work:** picking, adding and editing a leader change neither Updated nor urgency, and write nothing in Activity.
- **Backups and project files:** a backup and the data file carry the leaders with their portraits inline (`portraitUrl`); importing a backup replaces them, and one from before 0.1.51 brings none. Export this project carries its leader (name, role, color and portrait); importing it uses the city's leader of the same name, or adds it.
- **Data:** `leaders` in the database and `leader` on a project (section 3); portraits in the art store under `leader:` and the leader's id. A building whose leader is not in the city is left without one, on load and before every save (`tidyLeaders`).
- **The lines (0.1.58):** the second piece of the leader UX (section 6, Leader UX): `LEADER_LINES` in `23-leaders.js`, one shared set for every leader, and for Proxima when she brings a building's news, one line per moment, word for word as approved. `leaderSay(moment, values)` fills the blanks: a task's title cut short past 60 characters with an ellipsis (`LEADER_TITLE_MAX`), "{n} tasks" in the singular for one, a blank with nothing for it left as it is, and the line's first letter a capital. So far the leader card says its greeting (`leaderGreeting`): "Welcome back. Next up: <task>." with the next task (the pinned one first, as What is next), or "Nothing open here yet. Add the next task.", under the name and role, in a quote with a 2 px bar in its signature color, the full title as the tooltip when cut. It follows a tick or a pin, and is not work. Since 0.1.59 the other moments' lines are said as news (below). Nothing new is saved.
- **Clearer faces (0.1.57):** the first piece of the leader UX (section 6, Leader UX): the leader card's face at 96 px, the status card's at 48, and every speech bubble's at the map's top left at 100, Gus's and Proxima's now and the leaders' when they speak. The side bar's 48, the wheel's 64 and the toast's 40 stay. Framing and smooth shrinking are as before: no head crop and no second picture (D1). Since 0.1.75 a speech bubble's face is 64 px on a small screen, the words beside it (L2).
- **Alive (0.1.62):** the fifth and last piece of the leader UX (section 6, Leader UX, E1).
  - **A breathing face:** wherever a leader's face shows (News, its bubble, the status card, the leader card, the Leaders panel and its editor, the picker, the wheel's middle, the toast, Reports), its picture sits a pixel low in its frame, its foot under the frame's edge, and rises to its place for a third of each breath, in whole pixels. Each leader has its own slow rhythm, a breath every 3.4 to 5.4 seconds, from its id (`breathe` in `63-leaders.js`); its faces breathe together, kept to the page's clock, and a face drawn while hidden falls in step once it shows. A silhouette breathes too. Proxima breathes while she brings a building's news in the leaders' place (in News, her bubble there, and the status card of a building with no leader); Gus, and Proxima's own card and bubble, are as they were.
  - **Talking in its bubble:** when the leaders' bubble shows a line, it types out, a letter every 26 ms and a long line in 1.8 seconds at most, paced by the frame clock, while the face bobs a pixel faster (`face-talk`); then it settles and breathes. Every letter is in the line from the start, see-through until its turn, so the bubble keeps its size and a screen reader hears the line whole, once; the task stays a link while it types, and Arabic letters keep joined. Drawn again mid-line (a portrait loading, news joining), it carries on where it was; a line said already stays whole; Next types the next one, and a bubble opened again types its line again (`speak` in `77-news.js`).
  - **Motion on Still holds both:** the page wears `m-still` while Motion is Still, chosen or followed from the system (`Motion` in `30-pixel.js`), and the faces rest a pixel low; a line shows whole at once.
  - Nothing new is kept.

### The leaders' news (0.1.59)

The third piece of the leader UX (section 6, Leader UX): each leader brings its own buildings' news, news nobody else gives, in the shared lines; Proxima brings the news of a building with no leader, in the same lines. `77-news.js` (`News`) holds it.
- **The News section (N1, N3, G1, D1):** in the side bar, between This week and Due this week, where Urgent and Gus's part were. Gus first while the city has garbage, then each leader with news waiting (the most pressing first, then by name), then Proxima for the buildings with no leader. Each is a face at 48 px with the first word of its name under it (the whole name and role in its tooltip) and a badge in its color: ! for one item, else the count; Gus's counts his tasks between the city and clean streets. A click opens its bubble. Each leader's and Proxima's face has its own dismiss, shown while pointed at or focused, which reads its news unheard; there is no Dismiss all. The section hides with nothing in it. The Leaders tiles stayed below Due this week until the Leaders panel took their place at 0.1.61.
- **The moments (M1):**
  - Open by themselves: falling behind its pace (yellow, "We slipped behind our pace."; red, "We're falling further behind."; red under Work until finished, "Nothing finished here for a day."), each on the change, one at a time per building, the later replacing the earlier; tasks due today, on the day's first open, one per building ("{task} is due today." or "{n} tasks are due today. First up: {task}."); a tick that leaves a milestone of two tasks or more, with no repeat, one task from done, once a day; Claude adding tasks, notes or links ("Claude added 2 tasks, a note and 1 link."), with Undo; Claude attaching a wizard round, each with its own Undo, said by Quill since 0.1.69 (Leader bubbles, below); Claude Code waiting for you; Claude asking first, with the changes listed and Apply and Dismiss; Claude Code stopped on an error.
  - Wait with their leader: Claude Code finishing, with Tick it while the task a city copy started is still open; a round's answers coming in, with Open, said by Quill since 0.1.69, with Read too; the week, on the first open of a new Saturday-to-Friday week, one per building with a task to name ("Last week we finished 3 tasks. Next up: <task>." or "Quiet week: nothing finished. <task> is a good start."); the first week the city keeps news only starts counting.
  - Not news: a milestone finished (Proxima announces the party), Claude Code working (the status card only), the greeting (the leader card only), and a change you made yourself: picking a building's system or its days does not count as falling behind.
  - News that stops being true goes by itself: back on pace, nothing due today any more or a new day, a milestone no longer one task from done, a session no longer waiting or working again after an error, what Claude asked applied or dismissed, last week's news in a new week, a deleted building.
  - A line that needs a task and has none says nothing: a building behind its pace with nothing open, or the week with nothing open. A session with no task says "a task".
- **The bubble (B1, N3):** at the map's top left, where Gus and Proxima speak (`#ldSay`), beside the project view when it is open: the face at 100 px in a frame of its color, the name in its color and the role, the building with its picture (a button since 0.1.60: a click goes to it on the map, as Go to it does, and opens nothing), the line, what goes under it, and its buttons: Undo, Apply and Dismiss, Tick it or Open as the moment has them, then Go to it, which glides the map to the building. Its building wears the hover ring while it shows. Several items share one bubble, "1 of 3" with Next, the most pressing first: behind its pace, then Claude waiting, asking first or stopped, then due today, one from done, Claude's additions, finishes and answers, the week; news arriving while it shows joins it. Opened by itself it closes after 14 seconds and leaves its news waiting, unread; once clicked or used, or opened from a face (the focus moving into it), it stays until closed. Esc in it closes it. It waits while Gus or Proxima speaks, has something to say, or a panel is open, and gives way when either starts, its unread news coming back after them; Proxima waits for a bubble opened from a face.
- **The task as a link (0.1.60):** the task a line names shows in gold (#FFD27A), dotted underline, as a button: a click opens it in its project (`FullView.openTask`), found by the item's task id, else by its title in the building. From the bubble that reads the news and closes the bubble, as Open does, what else it held waiting; on the status card and the leader card it just opens the task. Abdurrahman asked for it on his first test, either Go to it opening the task or, better, the task in its own color and clickable; Go to it still goes to the building.
- **Pictures that load late (0.1.60):** portraits and a building's own art load from the art store a moment after the city opens, so a bubble that opened by itself at load showed the silhouette and the building's generic picture. Leaders' portraits loading (`Leaders.refreshAll`) now redraw the News faces and the bubble (`News.redraw`), and a building's art loading (`Art.onLoad`) redraws the bubble that names it.
- **Reading (D1):** an item is read once its bubble showed it and was closed, or Next went past it, or one of its buttons was used. What opened by itself and closed by itself never opens by itself again, after a reload too.
- **The status card (B3):** the leader's line under the building's name says its building's news, read as the card shows it and held while it stays open, and what Claude is doing there in the leader's voice, for as long as the card showed it before: one line a session, the most pressing first, at most four ("Claude is working on <task>. Started 12 minutes ago.", "Claude is waiting for you on <task>.", "Claude finished <task>." with the start of its reply and Tick it, "Claude stopped on an error while working on <task>."), and each change Claude asks about ("Claude wants your OK first: <change>. Sent just now."). Since 0.1.68 an update Claude sends is one report under its finish, a short line for each part with its own buttons (Claude's reports, below). A building with no leader has Proxima's face and name say them, and no line when there is nothing to say. Under the project view the card is hidden, so it reads nothing.
- **The leader card (B3):** in the project view, its building's news, read as it shows it and held while the view stays open, else its greeting. Since 0.1.69 it says what the status card says, with the same buttons, Claude at work included, and a building with no leader has Proxima say it there (Leader bubbles, below).
- **Toasts (T1):** Claude's inbox toast and the answers-in toast are now news. Toasts for your own actions stay, and so does the error for a change file the city cannot read or place.
- **Reports (B3):** the leader's face, 28 px, at the end of each building's row in By project, and 20 px before each building in a Proxima report's By building; none for a building with no leader.
- **Kept:** what was heard, how far behind each building was, the day due today was said and the week, in this browser (`nullovation-city:news`); several tabs share it. Undo lasts while the page stays open, as a toast's does. Nothing new in the city.

### Claude's reports (0.1.68)

From Claude's News on the Status Card, Round 1 (section 6), after one finished session on SwiftCoder filled more than half its status card with three items and about 20 lines. A1, A2, B3 and C2 are built; B1, B2 and C1 came back as questions. `News.report` and the views are in `77-news.js`; the changes one at a time in `69-inbox.js`.
- **One report (A1):** an update Claude sends (a change file in the inbox) is one report, said by its building's leader under the finish of the task it reports on: one item in News and in the leader's bubble, not three. Its task is the one the update names (`task`, the skill's new line), else the task its tick, description or round is for.
- **Waiting for the finish:** the update finds the session it came from: the one working on its task (or that finished it in the last 10 minutes); naming no task of its own, the one session working on the building, or the one that finished there in the last 2 minutes. While that session works, the report waits: what it adds is in the city at once, as before, but nothing is said, and the status card shows its parts under the working line. Once the session finishes, the finish carries the report and opens by itself while the report holds something to act on (an Undo, or a change waiting); a finish with nothing left to act on waits with its leader, as a finish does. When the session stops without a finish, or no session is found, the update is said on its own, as before: what it added, each round, and what it asks, each an item.
- **How it reads (A2):** the head line, "Claude finished <task>." ("Claude handed <task> back to you." since 0.1.69) with its one time ("Just now."); Claude's summary under it in its own words (C2): one line, in italics, after Claude's spark, cut short with the whole in its tooltip; then a short line for each part, never more than two lines, each with its own buttons:
  - "Added a note, Bed layout, and a link, Beds sketch." with Read and Undo. What was added is named: one of a kind by its name, more by their count.
  - "Attached a wizard round, <title>." (or "to <task>, <title>"), with Undo.
  - "Wants this task ticked off." (its own task) or "Wants <task> ticked off.", with Tick it and Dismiss; "Wants a new description for this task.", with Read, Apply and Dismiss; "Wants to replace the plan with 2 milestones and 9 tasks.", with Read, Apply and Dismiss. A change it cannot apply says why ("It is not open here.", "It is ticked off already.") with Dismiss alone.
  - A part's button keeps the report while it has something left: Undo takes its line away and the rest stays. In the bubble, acting on the last one goes on to the next item.
- **Tick it (B3):** under a report, only from Claude asking to tick the task off: an update that does not ask means work is left, so its finish has no Tick it. A finish with no update keeps Tick it, as before, with the start of Claude's reply as its words, its markdown taken off (`plainReply`).
- **Read:** a small panel over the city (`Panel.open` with `cr`, 560 px): what Claude added, each note's title and text whole, each task (a click opens it) with its description, each link with its address; a new description whole, then "Its description now"; a new plan's About and its milestones with their tasks. Esc closes it.
- **What asks first, on its own:** "Claude wants your OK first: <change>." names the change itself, never Claude's summary: "a new description for <task>", "tick off <task>" (", on to its next date" for a repeat), "a new plan", or "3 changes". Its time once, "Sent just now."; Claude's summary as its words; one change has its buttons on the line, several a short line each, each naming its task.
- **One change at a time:** Tick it, Apply and Dismiss act on their own change; the change is marked in its pending file (`_settled`: applied, skipped or dismissed), and the file moves to `inbox/done` once the last one goes. Toasts: a tick is its leader's ("Ticked off: <task>."), "Gave <task> its new description.", "Replaced the plan of <project>.", and for Dismiss "Left <task> open.", "Kept the description of <task>." or "Kept the plan of <project>.". "Claude: <summary>" went in Activity once per update until 0.1.68; since 0.1.69 Claude writes nothing there but a wizard round.
- **Kept:** reports in this browser with the rest of the news (`nullovation-city:news`, `reports`), for 13 hours, as long as a finish shows on the status card. Undo lasts while the page stays open. Nothing new in the city.

### Leader bubbles, redesigned (0.1.69)

From Leader Bubbles, Rounds 1 and 2 (section 6): every trigger of a leader's bubble designed on its own. 21 of the 31 triggers stay as they were; 10 change. Quill lives in `23-leaders.js` (`QUILL`, `quillLeader`) and `77-news.js` (`speakerFor`), the round's popup in `61-rounds.js` (`Rounds.view`), the overdue line in `20-model.js` (`logOverdue`).
- **Quill, the Pocket Wizard (C1):** the city's voice for wizard rounds, as Gus is for the garbage and Proxima for the reports. For every building he says a round Claude attached with no finish to sit under ("Claude attached a wizard round to <task>: <round>.", opening by itself, with Read and Undo) and a round's answers coming in ("Your answers are in for <round>: 7 of 8.", waiting in News, with Read and Open).
