His face shows in News apart from the building's leader, the plum #8E5A9E frame, his title The Pocket Wizard. Under a finish, a round is part of its leader's report, as before. His portrait is Abdurrahman's own art of him, shipped as `src/portraits/quill.png` (his 128 px drawing doubled pixel for pixel to 256), framed by its figure as a leader's is, and it breathes. A leader of the city's own named Quill is him: his rounds come under that leader's face, role and color, so one Quill shows in News, never two (Abdurrahman has him lead Decision Wizard).
- **A line another says on a card:** on a building's status card and leader card, a line Quill says starts with his face at 20 px and "Quill:" in his color, its bar in his color.
- **A round in a popup (C1):** Read on Quill's lines, and on a round in a Claude report, opens the round in a small panel over the city (`Panel.open` with `cr rd`): its kind and where it stands, the task and building it is for, its file, each question with its answer so far (Open, or Skipped once marked; a brainstorm card by card), and Open in wizard, which is Wizard it. Show the answers in the task panel's round card opens the same popup, in place of unfolding the answers in the card. Esc closes it.
- **The finish's words (C1):** "Claude handed <task> back to you.", on the status card, the leader card, the bubble and a report's head, since whether the task is done is Abdurrahman's to say.
- **Activity (C2):** Claude writes nothing in Activity but a wizard round it attaches ("Claude attached a wizard round to <task>: <title>"), whose Undo takes the line back out. Gone: "Claude Code finished: <task>" (0.1.37 to 0.1.68), "Claude: <summary>", "Claude wrote a note", "Claude added a link", "Claude updated the description of", "Undid what Claude added", and the "Added todo" and "New milestone" lines of Claude's add-tasks; the lines already written stay. A tick given from Claude's ask still writes its Done line, since it is a tick. A round's answers coming in write "Answers in for <round>: 7 of 8" (in cards seen for a brainstorm), each time Quill says them.
- **Overdue (N2):** a task going overdue writes "Overdue: <task>" in its project's Activity, dated the start of the day after its due date, in time order, once per task and due date (its `ref`, `overdue:<task>:<due>`), on opening the city and within a minute while it stays open. A done task or a repeat waiting for its date writes none; a line older than the 400 the log keeps is not written. Nobody says it, and it draws no crowd.
- **The leader card (N1):** in the project view it says what the status card says, read as it shows it, with the same buttons: Claude working, waiting, stopped and finished, a report with its parts, what Claude asks first with Tick it, Apply and Dismiss, and the building's news; with nothing to say, its greeting. A building with no leader has Proxima's face at 96 px say it there, with Pick a leader; with nothing to say, the card offers to pick a leader, as before.
- **Fixed:** a change Claude asked about, once applied or dismissed, and a session waiting for you, once it moves on, no longer linger on a card that read them.
- **Data:** nothing new in the city or the browser.

### All tasks and shifts (0.1.53)

The eighth of the task ideas (section 6, Tasks), designed in All tasks, Rounds 1 to 3, and the shifts' names asked in the chat (section 6, All tasks). `73-alltasks.js` holds the panel; `SHIFTS`, `SHIFT_HINTS` and `setShift` live in `20-model.js`, the + mark in `22-repeat.js`.
- **Shifts:** not what a task is, but how and when it can be done, in Abdurrahman's words. Create: at the screen and the keyboard, maybe with Claude working beside it, in work hours (code, art, testing). Think: pen and paper will do (design, writing, a decision; a decide task is a Think task, with or without a wizard round). Errand: away from the screen (daily tasks, buying things, a call). One per task or none (K3), and only these three: none can be added (K4).
  - **Setting it:** +create, +think or +errand in a task's line, or the start of one (+c, +t, +e); only + and a letter is a mark, and a + word that names none stays in the title, so "+music", "+1" and "C++" are left alone. The line under an add field says "Shift: Create", and empty it names the mark ("+create shift"). The task panel has a Shift row under Repeat: the three, the task's own pressed, a click on it taking it off; Esc undoes it as any edit there.
  - **Its tag:** a small pixel tag in the shift's color (Create teal #7FD6CE, Think lavender #B7A5F0, Errand lawn green #A9E2BE) on the project view's rows and the status card's rows, beside the repeat sign, and in What is next, at the right of the milestone's line before the due chip.
  - **Not work:** setting a shift changes neither Updated nor urgency, and writes nothing in Activity, like a pin.
  - **Claude:** Copy task and a milestone's copy say "Shift: Create"; the city summary gives each open task's `shift` in words; tasks files, plans and Claude's add-tasks may carry `shift`.
- **All tasks (L1, A1):** an All tasks button at the side bar's foot, beside Leaders over Reports and Settings, or L anywhere outside a field with no dialog open, or Due this week's "and N more" line, opens every open task in the city. Since 0.1.64 (SideBar Cleanup) it is a large panel at level 1, docked on the map's left like the project view and as wide (880 px), with the side bar's lists beside it; until then the side bar turned into it, sharing the side bar with Reports and Settings. Reports, Settings and the Leaders panel open beside it and leave it open; Esc closes it before them. Its close button, Esc, L or its button close it, the focus back on the button. A task opened from it opens the project view over it, in its place; once the view closes All tasks is back as it was, the focus on the row that opened it. Under the project view or a leader's editor, L or its button bring it to the front, closing them. Opening Edit City closes it. On a narrow window it covers the map, and the drawer closes. It opens with the focus on the panel, so L closes it again and the down arrow goes to the rows. It opens on everything each time: opening on the last shift picked was left out (L1).
- **Its top (D1, A2, L1):** the count line, "13 open, 1 overdue, 2 due this week", for what the list shows; a words field, Find a task, matching a task's title or description, its project's name or its milestone's, while nothing folds; a chip for each shift with its count (Create, Think, Errand), and No shift while there are untagged tasks; and a tile for each building with open tasks, by name after All, each counting the shift picked. A click on a chip or a tile shows only that, a second click (or All) everything; one of each at a time. With a shift picked, the rows drop their tags.
- **The list:** the starred task first, under Starred in a purple card with a gold edge, and not again below (B2); the dated tasks in day groups, Overdue, Today, Tomorrow, Rest of this week, Next week and Later, Saturday to Friday weeks, each with its count, by date and then by project (C1); then No due date: the undated tasks by building, those behind their pace first, the furthest behind first (its urgency garbage, `Garbage.breakdown(p).sys`), then by name (C2), each building's in its own order, the pinned task first, 3 each and then Show all N, which opens the rest in place; a building picked or words typed shows them all (C3).
- **A row (B1, D1):** the square, the task with its repeat sign, then its shift's tag, its date and where it lives: "Garden planner · First release" for a dated row, the milestone alone under a building. A late task says how late in red ("! 3 days overdue"); under Today and Tomorrow there is no chip; elsewhere it names the day ("Thu 22 Oct"). The pin and the Claude icon (Open in Claude, with Build it, Think and Copy; Copy task until 0.1.63) show while the row is pointed at or has the focus. Pointing at a row (or a tile) lights its building on the map with the hover ring, the map left where it is (`MapView.light`). The text opens the task in its project. The arrow keys move between rows (down from the field to the first, up from the first back to the field), Enter opens one and Space ticks it.
- **A tick:** as in the status card, the row stays struck through for a moment in its place, then goes; a repeat moves on, with its toast and Undo; its building's particles burst, through `tickTask`. A repeat waiting for its next date, done tasks and the archive stay out.
- **Sorting (L1):** No shift gathers the untagged tasks; pointing at an untagged row, or focusing it, shows the three shifts, and one click tags it; the focus goes on to the next row still to sort.
- **Kept up:** a change anywhere redraws it, but only when something it shows changed, so a row under the pointer stays put; the focus stays on the control that had it, the square included; the words field redraws once typing pauses. A ticked row keeps its place, its date, its star and its pin for its moment. The arrow keys and Space it handles stay in the list, so the map does not move with them. Tagging redraws an open project view too.
- **Its layout (0.1.64):** the count line, then the words field with the shift chips beside it, the building tiles, and the list in two columns when the panel has the room: the starred task and the day groups, and No due date with the buildings. A building selected while it is open glides into the open part of the map beside it, and the leaders' and Proxima's bubbles sit beside it, as beside the project view (`panelEdge` in `60-fullview.js`).
- **Left out (D1, L1):** an add field and a date button on each row; opening on the last shift picked, Copy and Open in Claude per building, and a switch to group by shift.
- **Data:** a task may carry `shift`, one of `create`, `think` or `errand`; reading a city keeps only those.

### Claude's notes, and notes in a task (0.1.71)

From Claude's Notes, Rounds 1 and 2 (section 6). The model is in `20-model.js` (`NOTE_ICONS`, `NOTE_KINDS`, `KIND_ICON`, `readNote`, `allNotes`, `noteTask`, `taskNotesForClaude`), Claude's add-note in `69-inbox.js`, the cards, the panel and a task's notes in `60-fullview.js`, the icons in `30-pixel.js`.
- **Notes in a task (K1, Y1):** a task can hold notes. They show in its panel under the description, newest first (by when they were last brought up to date), each with its icon, its title, two lines of it and when; Claude's with its spark and By Claude. A click opens one in the note panel, which says "In <task>" with Back to the task; Done, Back to the task, Esc and Delete note go back to the task. Add a note writes one of yours there, starting with the sticky note; it writes "Wrote a note in <task>: <title>" in Activity, as an edit or a delete writes theirs. An archived task's notes are to read: a click shows one whole. Turn into a task is for a note in Notes only. A task's copy carries its notes, under "## Notes on this task", so the next session on it starts from them; the city summary gives an open task its `notes`. A task turned into a note takes its own notes into Notes, and Undo puts them back. Esc in the task panel leaves a note Claude put in the task while it was open.
- **Claude's notes (K1, K2, T1):** an update's add-note names its `kind`, and the city puts it in its place: a status (where a long piece of work stands), a plan and a decision go in the task they are about, the note's own `task`, else the update's; how something works goes in Notes; a status, a plan or a decision with no task here goes in Notes too, and so does a note of no kind, as before. A status and a plan stay one note in their task, and how something works one note per title in Notes: sent again, Claude's note is brought up to date, its title and text replaced, and it says when; a decision is a new note each time. Claude only ever changes a note of its own. Its icon is the one it sends, else its kind's: a status the sticky note, a plan the blueprint, a decision the idea bulb, how something works the book. Its leader says it in the report: "Added a note, <title>." or "a new version of <title>", with Read, which says the task it is in, and Undo, which takes a note out of its task or puts the version before back.
- **Icons (M1, I1):** every note has one of four, picked in its panel and changed any time, Claude's too: the sticky note, the idea bulb, the book (as in rules) and the blueprint (a plan; the map redrawn at his ask). The panel shows the four as icon buttons, the one picked pressed with its name beside them. Picking one is not work: Updated and urgency stay, and Activity writes nothing. A note from before has the sticky note.
- **Claude's mark (M1):** a note Claude wrote shows its spark and By Claude on its card and in its panel, which says "By Claude, <when>." and, once brought up to date, when. Its copy says so too.
- **Its lines (L1):** a note's card keeps its lines, so a numbered list reads as one, three lines at most.
- **Search, the recap, Proxima and the builder card** read a task's notes too.
- **Data:** a task may carry `notes`; a note may carry `icon` (one of `NOTE_ICONS`), `by: 'claude'`, `kind` (one of `NOTE_KINDS`) and `updatedAt`. Reading a city keeps only those.

### Tasks into notes, and back (0.1.54)

Abdurrahman's task "Turn tasks into Ideas(Notes)" in Task Improvements: some tasks are better kept as open ideas (section 6, Tasks into notes). Both live in `60-fullview.js`.
- **Turn into a note:** at the foot of a task's panel, left of Delete task. The panel closes as it always does, its Done and edit lines first; the task leaves its milestone, and a note takes its title, and its description as the note's text, written now. Its milestone, due date, repeat, shift, pin and star belong to a task, so they go. The note opens in the panel in the task's place, its card picked and the focus in its text. A title longer than a note's 200 characters is not cut: the note has no title and opens with it, the description after it. Every task in the lists has the button, a done one too; an archived task is to read, so it has none until it is restored.
- **Turn into a task:** at the foot of a note's panel, left of Delete note, whose line on when it was written now sits under them. The note becomes a task where one added without @ goes: the current milestone, else the last one not in the archive, else a new To do (`homeMilestone`), at its end, open, with no date. Its title names it, or its first line when it has none, the rest describing it. The task opens in the panel in the note's place, its row picked. The button's tooltip names the milestone; an empty note has nothing to turn, so it waits for words.
- **Undo:** each shows a toast, "Turned Water the beds into a note." or "Turned Seed library into a task in First release.", with Undo, which puts back exactly what was there: the task in its place with everything it carried, its pin and the star too unless another task took them since, or the note with its date. A To do made for it goes again, and so do the Activity lines it wrote. While the panel still shows what it became, the original opens there again, the focus on its Turn button. Esc in the panel that opens undoes only what was typed since it opened.
- **Activity and counts:** "Turned into a note: <title>" and "Turned into a task: <title>". Neither is work: urgency stays, while Updated moves, as for any new task or note. The week's recap counts a note turned into a task as a task added (`addedTodo` in `65-recap.js`), and a task turned into a note as a note written, by its date. A haunting task turned into a note lays its ghost to rest within a second.
- **Data:** nothing new.

### Wizard rounds in a task (0.1.55)

The third of the task ideas (section 6, Tasks), from Tasks, Round 2, W1 to W6, designed in Wizard Rounds in a Task, Rounds 1 to 3 (section 6, Wizard rounds in a task). A round is a questions file for Abdurrahman's Decision Wizard, which Claude writes with the d-wizard skill. `21-rounds.js` holds the model, `61-rounds.js` the card, the hats, the files and the answers.
- **Kept in the task (W2, W6):** a task holds one round: its title, its file's name, the folder it names, its questions, its answers as they come, and whether it was marked answered. A new round replaces the old one; one with the same title updates it. A ticked, waiting or archived task keeps its round and wears no hat.
- **Asking for one (W1, M1, and since 0.1.63 the Think tab):** Open in Claude on a task has the Think tab beside Build it (Open in Claude, below; a second row, Make a wizard round, from 0.1.55 to 0.1.62), with Chat, Cowork, Code, Terminal and Copy, Code and Terminal needing a folder link. Each sends the task's copy ending with `ROUND_LINE`: plan this task further with a decision wizard round written with the d-wizard skill, asking only what is still open, then send it with the nullovation-city skill so it attaches to this task (from a chat, as a file), and do not start the task itself yet.
- **Coming back:** from Claude Code, the skill writes the round into `wizard-rounds` in the Claude folder and sends an `add-round` change (Claude reads and updates the city, below), which applies at once with a toast, Undo and "Claude attached a wizard round to <task>: <title>" in Activity. From a chat or anywhere else, the round file is dropped on the task's panel: while a file is dragged over it, a dashed teal line lights up, "Drop the round file to attach it to this task", or "Drop a round or its answers file here" once it has one (P2); nothing shows otherwise. A drop says "Attached <round> to <task>." (adding "It replaces <old>." for another round), with Undo; with the Claude folder connected the file is written into `wizard-rounds` too.
- **The card (P1, P1a):** in the task panel, under Pin as next and Star for the city: the hat, Wizard round and where it stands ("Waiting: 3 of 8 answered", "Answered: 8 of 8", "Answered: 3 of 8, 5 skipped"), the round's title, its file (`wizard-rounds\<file>`), Wizard it (Open in wizard once answered), Mark answered while some but not all are answered, and Remove. With answers, Show the answers unfolded them, each question by its id, its title and question, and its answer, Open or Skipped, with batch rows, follow-ups and notes; since 0.1.69 it opens them in the round's popup (Leader bubbles, above). Answered, the card has a teal edge. An archived task's card is to read: Wizard it alone. Esc in the panel leaves the round as it is, since it comes from outside.
- **Wizard it (W3, F1, rethought at 0.1.65 and 0.1.66):** opens the wizard on the task's round, in the installed wizard app or in a tab, as Settings, Links says (Wizard app, the default, or Browser tab; `nullovation-city:wizardOpen`, 0.1.66).
  - **In the app (0.1.66):** with the Claude folder connected, the hosted wizard and Wizard app picked, the round's file goes into `wizard-rounds` first, then Wizard it follows a `web+dwizard:` link, which the app registers with Windows (`protocol_handlers` in its manifest, wizard 0.11.2), so it opens from any browser: a link clicked in one browser never reaches an app installed from another, and Abdurrahman runs the city in Maxthon with the app installed from Chrome. Windows takes about 2,000 characters, so the link names the round rather than carrying it: `web+dwizard:open/` and base64url JSON, `{ file, title }` (`appLink`). The app opens it at `?open=` and the link, reads the file from its connected folder (the newest version, its answers saving there), else opens the round of that title from its list, else asks to be let into a folder it knows, or says which file and folder to open once. The toast says "Opening <round> in your wizard app.", the first time adding "The first time, give the app the Claude folder's wizard-rounds folder, and rounds open there by themselves.", with Open in a tab, which opens the tab route. The first time, the browser and the app may each ask before opening the link.
  - **In a tab:** with Browser tab picked, an address of your own, or no Claude folder: a link to the wizard's address, clicked for you, to a new tab (`target="_blank"`, `rel="noopener"`), not `window.open`. Chrome and Edge take such a link into the app when the city runs in the same browser (tested at 0.1.66: every kind of link, the app set to open in its own window); Maxthon 7.5 never does, even with the wizard installed in Maxthon and its link capturing on. Until 0.1.64 it opened in a tab of its own, the same one each time.
  - **On this round:** the link carries it after `#open=`, as base64url JSON: `{ app, kind: 'open-round', file, round, answers }`, the round, its answers so far and its file's name (`openLink`). The wizard opens it whatever it had open: a round it has opens with its answers, a changed version replaces it as Load would, and a new one comes in with the answers sent along. Until 0.1.64 the address named no round, so the wizard showed the round it had open last.
  - **The answers:** with the Claude folder connected it makes sure the round's file is in `wizard-rounds`, writing it again if it is gone, and the wizard, given that folder once, saves the answers into it as you go. A round that names its folder (Claude Code's) asks for it in the wizard; one from a chat names none, and the wizard saves into a folder it already has that holds the file. The first time, the toast says "Opening <round> in your wizard. Give it the Claude folder's wizard-rounds folder once, and your answers come back here as you go.", then "Opening <round> in your wizard." With no Claude folder: "Once it is answered, save its file there and drop it on this task." Nothing downloads any more: until 0.1.64 the round's file downloaded to load by hand.
- **The answers (W4):** the tab that writes the data file reads each round's file with the rest of the Claude folder, every few seconds, and takes answers that belong to the round (the wizard names it, or names nothing). Once they stop arriving for 4 seconds its building's news says "Your answers are in for <round>: 7 of 8." with Open, which opens the task (since 0.1.59; a toast until then), said by Quill with Read too, and written in Activity, since 0.1.69. An answers file dropped on the task works the same way; answers to another round are refused, naming it.
- **Answered (A1, S1):** a round counts as answered when nothing is open, or once Mark answered is pressed, which counts the open questions as skipped on purpose ("Marked answered: 5 questions skipped.", with Undo).
- **A brainstorm (0.1.63, Open in Claude Panel A3):** Think with Make a brainstorming round ticked sends the task's copy ending with `BRAINSTORM_LINE`: explore this task with a brainstorm written with the d-wizard skill, its round 1, or its round 2 when the task's brainstorm has round 1 answered, sent with the nullovation-city skill so it attaches to this task (from a chat, as a file), not starting the task itself. A brainstorm is the wizard's two-round six-hats form (`config.kind` brainstorm, `config.round` 1 or 2, a `topic`, and in round 1 `hold`, `sparks`, `suggest` and `recap`, in round 2 `aims`, `extras`, `ideas` and `order`), and it attaches like a round: dropped on the task's panel, or by Claude Code's add-round, the same file and answers handling, the same hat. Round 2 replaces round 1, as any new round does. Its card says "Brainstorm, round 1" or "Brainstorm, round 2" and counts cards seen ("Waiting: 5 of 17 cards seen"): the wizard's `meta.total` and `meta.seen` once answers come, before that the cards it holds (round 1: the hats' turns, or 6, each hat with sparks, the suggestions, or 6, and the recap; round 2: the aims, the extras when it has any, each idea and each extra, and the order). It counts as answered once its last card (the recap, or the order) is seen, or with nothing left, or marked ("Marked answered: 12 cards skipped."). A copy says "## Brainstorm, round N: <title>" and "Waiting for answers (15 cards)", "5 of 17 cards seen" or "Answered, 16 of 17 cards seen"; for a chat it writes the answers in card by card: each hat's turn with your cards, what you killed, the sparks and what they set off, the suggestions kept or killed, and in round 2 the extras, each idea on the council with your rewrite, the changes, answers, ideas taken and variants, and your order (`brainstormAnswerLines`). The city summary's `round` has `kind`, `brainstorm` or `round`.
- **The hat (W5, A2, H1, X1):** the Decision Wizard's own hat, a cone and brim, flat and in one color: gray (#8A83A3) while its round waits, teal (#2DB5A9) once answered. It shows on an open task's row in the project view (after the description sign), the status card and All tasks, in the hover's next-task line, and in What is next as a chip beside Pinned and Starred, "4 of 8" or "Answered". A click on it is Wizard it, and neither opens nor drags the task.
- **Claude:** Copy task adds a section, `## Wizard round: <title>`, where it stands ("Waiting for answers (8 questions)", "3 of 8 answered", "Answered, 8 of 8", "3 of 8 answered, 5 skipped on purpose"), and where its file is: the full path when the round named its folder, else `wizard-rounds/<file>` in the Claude folder that `~/.claude/nullovation-city.json` names (A2). Sent to a chat, or from a computer with no Claude folder, the copy writes the answers in instead, one line each: "- B1, Bed layout: How should the beds be laid out? A grid. Note: keep the middle path wide." (C1). Cowork gets the round's file attached when its full path is known. A milestone's copy says "Wizard round: <title> (3 of 8 answered)" under the task; the city summary gives each open task's `round` (title, file, state).
- **Remove:** takes the round off the task with Undo, "Took <round> off this task. Its file stays in the folder."
- **Not work:** dropping, answering, marking and removing change neither Updated nor urgency, and write nothing in Activity; Claude's add-round writes its line, since 0.1.69 the answers coming in write theirs, and since 0.1.70 a round or its answers dropped by hand write theirs: "Attached a wizard round to <task>: <title>", "Updated the wizard round of <task>: <title>" for the same round dropped again with nothing new answered, and "Answers in for <round>: 3 of 8" for the answers it brought; the drop's Undo takes them back out.
- **Data:** a task may carry `round`: `{ title, file, folder, q, a, marked, at }`, `q` the round as the wizard reads it and `a` its answers. Reading a city keeps a whole round only, and none past 300,000 characters (`ROUND_MAX`). Settings, Links' wizard address and whether its first hint was said are kept in this browser.

### The status bubble

Click a building for a small card:
- its leader's face, name and role under the building's name, when it has one (0.1.51), and what it says: the building's news and Claude's lines in its voice, Proxima's for a building with no leader (0.1.59);
- the project's next 3 open tasks: the pinned one first (0.1.39), then those due soon;
- tasks can be ticked off from it, with a short animation; a repeat moves on to its next date (0.1.42);
- a task with a wizard round wears its hat, gray or teal, which opens the round in the wizard (0.1.55);
- until 0.1.76, an add field, Add a task, with the one-line marks (0.1.42); since then a task is added with + Task, Enter or T on the line over the map, or inside the building (The City on a Phone, Round 2, A3), and a card with nothing open says "No todos yet. Add one with + Task, or enter the building.";
- when the project was last updated;
- an Enter button that steps inside.

### The project view (stepping inside)

- **Top:** the building's picture, with the leader card under it since 0.1.51 (Leaders, above), and the About: the project's description first, then What is next, with Open in Claude for the next task (and Copy task beside it until 0.1.62). Since 0.1.39 What is next shows the pinned task when there is one; since 0.1.40 it is a widget of its own, a shade lighter than the view; since 0.1.41 it is laid out as layout B, purple with a gold edge while its task wears the star (Tasks on the map, above).
- **Milestones and tasks:** tasks are grouped by milestone. They can be reordered by dragging, and each has an optional due date, an optional repeat (0.1.42) and a description. A milestone's + opens a one-line add field with the marks (0.1.42). A task opens in a side panel, with Open in Claude (and Copy task until 0.1.62), since 0.1.54 Turn into a note (Tasks into notes, and back, above), and since 0.1.55 its wizard round's card under Pin and Star, its hat on its row (Wizard rounds in a task, above). Since 0.1.49 archived tasks leave the lists, a done row has an archive button, and a milestone's foot counts its archived tasks (The archive, above).
- **Milestone view:** clicking a milestone's name opens its rename field and its tasks as a read-only list. Done tasks are ticked and struck through, and open ones show their due date and the first line of their description; archived ones show dimmed under the rest, with the archive's icon (0.1.49). It also has Open in Claude (with Copy for Claude beside it until 0.1.62), Archive done tasks (0.1.49) and Delete milestone.
- **Links:** since 0.1.54 the first section in the column beside Todos, level with it and above Notes, since the city runs on them. Each link shows an icon: Doc, Code, GitHub, Claude, Chat, Folder or Web. An address picks its own icon as it is typed: claude.ai and claude:// get Claude, github.com GitHub, and a Windows path Folder. With the setting on, folder links open in File Explorer. (Until 0.1.26 there were Task and Design icons; links saved with them get an icon picked from their address.)
  - **Add link** opens a panel the width of the column: the icon, the address, and the label on its own line under it, then Add to shortcuts, on by default.
  - **Paste to add (0.1.27):** Ctrl+V with an address on the clipboard, while the project view is open and no field is in use, opens Add link with it filled in, ready for a label. Text that is not an address (one line starting with a scheme and //, mailto:, www., or a Windows path) is left alone.
  - **A label from the address (0.1.27):** the label field offers a name read from the address alone, since the tool works offline: a GitHub repository's name (with #12 for an issue or pull request), a folder's or file's own name, Claude project, Claude chat or Claude, a github.io page's first folder, an app's name (Slack), or the site's name (figma.com). Tab in the empty field takes it, to edit. A link without a label keeps it empty and shows that name, on its button and in the wheel, instead of its whole address. `suggestLabel` in `20-model.js`.
  - **The same address twice (0.1.27):** an address the project already links, by `addressKey` (case, a trailing slash, www, and http or https aside), says so under the address with that link's name, and Add becomes Add anyway.
  - **Editing** the section shows each link as a row with a grip, its icon, the address over the label, a Shortcut switch, and delete. A link drags by its grip to a new place, or moves with the arrow keys on the grip; the list's order is the wheel's order. Deleting one shows a toast with Undo for 6 seconds, which puts it back in its place, while the section is open or after Done (0.1.27).
  - **Past 8 shortcuts** a note says only 8 fit in a building's wheel, so the first 8 in the list show. More are allowed.
- **Notes:** under Links (above them until 0.1.54). Notes show as cards, each with its own copy button. A note opens in a panel with Open in Claude (and Copy for Claude until 0.1.62), Turn into a task (0.1.54) and Delete.
- **The shortcut wheel (0.1.26):** a right-click on a building (a long press on a phone, since 0.1.75) opens a pie of its shortcuts round the pointer, like the Sims' pie menu: its links marked as shortcuts that can be opened, in the links' order, at most 8, the first at the top and the rest clockwise. The middle is a round frame kept for the leader's face, which shows there since 0.1.51, and the building's name sits above. A shortcut opens like its link in the project view. Esc, a click elsewhere, scrolling, or a click on a shortcut closes it; the arrow keys move between shortcuts. A building with none says so. It does not select the building, and opens nothing on a park, while planting or moving, or in Edit City.
  - **Since 0.1.27:**
    - Its buttons fit about three words (up to 216 px), each growing outward from the ring: left-aligned on the right, right-aligned on the left, centered only near the top and the bottom. Since 0.1.74 a wheel wider than the map, as on a phone, stands on a smaller ring (76 px from the middle, against 112), the labels at its sides cut short to fit and those at the top and the bottom keeping their room.
    - Enter, a button under the wheel, steps inside the building, as the bubble's Enter does. The wheel opens with no shortcut picked, so the Enter key steps inside too; the arrow keys pick a shortcut, and Enter then opens it.
    - F, with a building selected, opens its wheel round the building, and F again closes it.
    - The wheel and the status bubble never show together: a right-click closes the bubble, and a click on a building closes the wheel and opens the bubble. The empty middle lets a click through to the building under it.
    - With the wheel open, the arrow keys and WASD leave the map still, and Q and E hop on from the wheel's building.
- **The more menu:** Archive, with its count (0.1.49), Pick a leader or Leader: <name> (0.1.51), Copy for builder, Builder card in a Claude chat, Load plan or tasks (JSON from the nullovation-city skill), Export this project, and Delete project.
- **The building:** clicking its picture opens the building panel, which picks one of the generic buildings or uploads the project's own art as a PNG or GIF, and since 0.1.46 picks its particles (Celebrations, above). Under the picture, Mirror flips the building and Download saves it as a GIF.
- **The urgency system:** chosen per project.

### The side bar

- **Top:** the logo, with the version in very small text under it. Saving that is paused or has failed (the browser, the data file, the daily backups) shows just under it with its fix, over the lists and Settings alike, and so does a Claude folder that is paused or missing.
- **Lists:** New project, which you plant on a free plot, and + Task right after it (0.1.76), which opens the line over the map, both stepping aside while a new project is named; search, which reads names, descriptions, tasks (archived ones too), milestones, notes, and the links' labels and addresses (addresses since 0.1.27); the starred task, while there is one (0.1.40); This week (the weekly recap), its numbers each with a small icon since 0.1.64, a tick for tasks done, a note for notes and a flag for milestones ("Open the week" under them until 0.1.63; the card still opens the week); Status brief under it, one button (0.1.64; Open in Claude until 0.1.63, and Copy status brief beside it until 0.1.62) whose panel starts with Copy status brief, then Chat, Cowork, Code and Terminal: the status brief is a summary of every project for a Claude chat; News, with Gus while the city has garbage and the leaders with news (0.1.59, in the place of Gus's part, 0.1.32, and Urgent); and Due this week, each task with the Claude icon (0.1.64; a copy button until then), which opens Open in Claude with Build it, Think and Copy, and whose "and N more" line opens All tasks (0.1.53). The Leaders section (0.1.51) left for the Leaders panel at 0.1.61.
- **The foot (0.1.43):** four buttons of one size, two by two since 0.1.64: All tasks and Leaders, then Reports and Settings. Reports (0.1.43) and Settings turn the side bar into themselves, and so does Leaders (0.1.61); All tasks (0.1.53) opens on the map since 0.1.64.
- **Levels (0.1.64, SideBar Cleanup):** the side bar is level 0: its lists, and Leaders, Reports and Settings in their place one at a time. What holds too much for it opens at level 1, a panel docked on the map's left like the project view: the project view, All tasks, and a leader's editor. The project view opens over All tasks, and a leader's editor over both; each closes back to what it covered.

### Settings (0.1.24)

The side bar turns into Settings, and back with its arrow, so the map stays in view and shows every change as it is made. It opens from the button at the foot of the side bar, the gear on the map's bar, or the comma key; Esc closes it, after ending Edit City. On a narrow window the gear opens the drawer on Settings.
- **The header:** the title, the back arrow, and a small ? that shows the controls guide: move, zoom, status, hop, shortcuts (right-click or F), step inside (a double-click, or F then Enter, since 0.1.56; Enter until then), add a task (+ Task since 0.1.76, Enter on the map since 0.1.56, or T, since 0.1.42), All tasks (L, since 0.1.53), Reports (R, since 0.1.43), Settings (the comma key) and Esc. It leaves out moving buildings, which Edit City's bar teaches (0.1.25). On a touch screen it shows a phone's own guide instead (0.1.75, S1): drag, pinch, tap, hold, double tap, + Task in the menu with the keys under its line (Add a task on a card until 0.1.76), the menu and back.
- **Tabs:** six pixel-icon tabs, City, Life, Look, Projects, Saving and Links, with the tab's name under them; arrow keys move between them.
- **Ground rules:**
  - Changes apply at once and are saved, with no toast; actions such as Export, Download palette and Test folder links keep theirs.
  - A dot marks a setting that differs from its default, and its tab.
  - Each tab has Reset to defaults, except Saving, whose data file and backups folder are connections rather than preferences. Reset all settings, at the foot, asks first. Resets never touch the projects, the map, or the parks picked in Edit City.
  - It opens again on the tab, and at the place on the page, where it was left.
  - Traffic, Bubbles and Time of day are on the bar and in Settings, with the same choices and icons, in step.
- **City:** the map's size, with the Edit City button; street props on or off, each kind on or off, How many, from a quarter to twice today's props, and Deal again.
  - How many: each prop has its own draw, so more only adds props and fewer only takes some away; none moves. Bus stops keep their pattern up to today's amount, and above it some stand off it.
  - Deal again lays the props out anew by the same rules, kept until dealt again.
  - At today's amount with no new deal, the plan is today's.
- **Life:** Traffic; who is out: cars, buses, drones, cyclists, walkers; two sliders from a quarter to twice today's counts, Street traffic (cars, buses and drones) and Walkers (people and cyclists); and Crowds round busy buildings. Walkers kept in takes the crowds too; with bus stops off, the buses drive past.
- **Look:** Time of day; Auto's hours, when night starts (19:00) and day starts (06:30), with dusk and dawn blending over the 90 minutes before each; Bubbles; Garbage (0.1.31), on or off; Ghosts (0.1.47), on or off; Motion; Postcard, the caption on or off, as on screen or doubled; and Download palette.
  - Motion: Full; Saver, at most 30 frames a second; or Still: no traffic, every building and park on its first frame, and bubbles that do not bob. Until one is chosen it follows the system: Still when the system asks for reduced motion.
- **Projects:** rest days, picked by weekday from Saturday, and away until a date (with Clear).
  - Rest days count for neither One task per N days nor Work until finished; a building's reason says so.
  - Before the away date no system turns a building yellow or red; from that day every system counts from its start. Due dates count every day, rest or away.
  - Away also stops a late task's garbage growing (0.1.31): the city keeps the days away, from the day it is set to the day you are back, and Clear ends them today.
- **Saving:** the app, first, since 0.1.67 (The app, below); the data file; daily backups; Export and Import; and storage use: how much of about 5 MB the projects and settings take in this browser, with a bar and a warning near the limit, and what the building art and the rest take.
  - Daily backups (Chrome and Edge): a folder picked once. The first time the city is open each day, before the day's changes, it saves `nullovation-city-daily-YYYY-MM-DD.json` there and keeps the newest 14. Only files with that name are counted and pruned, so exported backups in the same folder are never touched. One tab writes, the one the data file uses. Paused or failed shows under the logo too, with Allow or Pick the folder again.
- **Links:** folder links in File Explorer, Get the setup, and Test folder links, which opens C:\Windows through a folder link; since 0.1.55 Decision wizard, your wizard's address, which Wizard it opens (the hosted wizard when empty; Wizard rounds in a task, above), and since 0.1.66 whether Wizard it opens the Wizard app or a Browser tab; and since 0.1.28 Claude Code at work (above).
- **Where each setting lives:** what the city is like travels in the data file and every backup: crowds, who is out, the sliders, street props, Deal again, the parks picked, Auto's hours, rest days, and away until with the days away. How this computer shows it stays in this browser: the Traffic, Bubbles and Time of day buttons, the camera, folder links, the data file, the daily backups' folder, motion, the garbage and ghosts switches, the postcard, and the wizard's address (0.1.55) and whether it opens in the app or a tab (0.1.66).

### Edit City (0.1.24)

Opened from Settings, City. A bar over the map shows the size, Grow, Shrink and Done, and the map frames the whole city.
- **Lines:** each side has an arrow out that adds a line of plots on that side, while the city is under 11 plots that way, and an arrow in that takes off that side's outer line, only when the line is empty and the city is over 5 plots that way. So the city can be a rectangle, 5 to 11 plots each way. The arrows follow the map, and on a city bigger than the view they stay at its edge.
- **Rings:** Grow and Shrink, as before: a ring all round, while both ways stay within 5 to 11; Shrink only when the ring is empty.
- **Parks:** pointing at a park names its kind, and a click deals the next one. The picks travel with the city, kept by their place, so a line taken off and added back brings its picks back. Round all seven, back at the kind its place deals, the pick is dropped.
- **Buildings:** a plain drag moves one, with no hold, and a click does nothing. Outside Edit City, moving stays hold then drag with a mouse; on a touch screen a long press opens the wheel, so buildings move in Edit City only (0.1.75, G1).
- **Everything stays with its buildings:** a line or a ring moves the grid, not the city. Each plot keeps its place in the whole city, so parks, street details, crossings and props stay beside their buildings, and the view stays on them.
- Esc, Done, closing Settings, or planting a project ends it. Q and E do not hop in it.

### The weekly recap

The recap keeps one snapshot per Saturday-to-Friday week, and you can go back to any older week.

### Copy for Claude formats

No copy carries the project's About any more (until 0.1.26 every copy ended with it). Since 0.1.63 every copy is made with Copy in Open in Claude's panel, which says what it copied ("Task copied for Claude.", "Note copied for Claude.", "Milestone copied for Claude, with its tasks.", "Status copied. Paste it into Claude.", "Status brief copied. Paste it into any Claude chat."); until 0.1.62 tasks had Copy task, notes and milestones Copy for Claude, and the project and the side bar Copy status and Copy status brief. A note's card keeps its small copy icon.

| Copy | Opens with | Then |
|---|---|---|
| Task | `# Task: <title>` | Project, milestone, due date, the repeat if any, the shift if any (0.1.53), state (open, done, done and in the archive, or ticked and coming back on a date), the description, and its wizard round with where it stands and where its file is, or its answers written in for a chat (0.1.55) |
| Note | `# Note: <title>` | Project, when it was written, and the note |
| Milestone | `# Milestone: <name>` | Project and progress (archived tasks counted); every open task with its due date, repeat and full description; the repeats waiting for their next date; then the done tasks as a list, those in the archive left out (0.1.49) |
| Builder card | `# Nullovation City builder card` | Project name and size signals (open and done tasks, milestones, notes, links), description, milestones, open and done tasks. Abdurrahman may add his own idea for the building under it. |
| Status brief | `# Nullovation City status, <date>` | Every project at once |

Since 0.1.51 a task's, a note's and a milestone's copies, and a project's status (so the status brief), carry `Leader: <name>, <role>` after the project when the building has a leader.

Since 0.1.36 every copy for Claude (a task, a note, a milestone, a project's status, the status brief) ends with `REPORT_LINE`, "When you finish, send Nullovation City an update with the nullovation-city skill." The builder card does not. Since 0.1.55 a task's copy sent by Think (Make a wizard round until 0.1.62) ends with `ROUND_LINE` instead, and since 0.1.63 with Make a brainstorming round ticked, `BRAINSTORM_LINE` (Wizard rounds in a task, above).

### Open in Claude

Open in Claude wears the Claude icon (the chat icon until 0.1.26): in the task, milestone and note panels, on What is next, in an archived task's panel, beside the project's status in the About, as Status brief under This week in the side bar (0.1.64), and since 0.1.64 as the Claude icon alone on each task in Due this week and All tasks. Since 0.1.63 it opens a small panel (Open in Claude Panel, Round 1, and Abdurrahman's sketch), a menu until then. It opens the Claude desktop app through the app's `claude://` links, or Claude Code in a terminal through its own `claude-cli://` link, with the same text filled in and ready to send:
- **Five tiles in a row:** Chat, marked "no folder"; Cowork, with its own icon since 0.1.63 (two figures; the folder icon is for folders); Code; Terminal; and Copy, which only copies the text and says what it copied (A1). The status brief's panel (0.1.64) puts Copy first, as a wide row, Copy status brief, with the four Claude tiles under it. On a touch screen, since 0.1.75, the panel is Copy alone, as a wide row, with a task's Build it and Think over it, and no folder (B4): the desktop app's and the terminal's links cannot open on a phone.
  - **Chat:** a new chat.
  - **Cowork:** a new task in the panel's folder. Since 0.1.63 one folder only, and no file links, except a task's round file, attached when its full path is known (Wizard rounds in a task, above); until then every local folder and file link of the project.
  - **Code:** a new Claude Code session in the panel's folder. Without a folder link it is greyed out, saying it needs one.
  - **Terminal (0.1.37):** Claude Code in a new terminal (Windows Terminal first, then PowerShell, then cmd) in the panel's folder: `claude-cli://open?q=<text>&cwd=<folder>`. Its own icon, a terminal with a prompt. Greyed out without a folder on this computer: a network path or one with `..` does not count, though Code still opens there. Claude Code registers the link the first time it gets a prompt in a terminal on the computer.
- **The folder (0.1.63):** under the tiles, "Folder: <path>": the project's first local folder link. With two or more, the line is a button with > (∨ while open) that lists them, each by its name over its full path; the one picked is kept for that building in this browser (`nullovation-city:claudeFolder`), and picking the first again forgets it. With none: "No folder link: Code and Terminal need one." A copy with no project, the status brief, says "No project folder: Chat, Cowork or Copy."
- **Build it and Think (0.1.63, A2):** a task's panel, in the task panel and What is next while the task is open, has two tabs over the tiles: Build it, with the Claude icon, picked each time it opens, which sends the task to do; and Think, with the wizard's hat, which sends the task's copy asking for a decision wizard round about it, or with its checkbox, Make a brainstorming round (unticked each time), a brainstorm (Wizard rounds in a task, above). The tiles and the folder are the same in both; Copy copies what that tab sends ("Copied: asking Claude for a wizard round on this task." or "...a brainstorm on this task."). A done task, a note, a milestone, a project's status and the status brief have no tabs. From 0.1.55 to 0.1.62 a second row, Make a wizard round, did Think's job. A copy's text can differ by where it goes: a chat gets a round's answers written in.
- **Keys:** the focus goes to the tab picked, else the first tile it can use; the arrow keys move along the tabs or the tiles; Esc, a click elsewhere, scrolling the page or resizing it close the panel, the focus back on the button. At most 344 px wide, narrower on a narrow window.

Nothing is sent by itself: Claude shows the text and waits. The text also goes on the clipboard, for a computer without the app. The app fills in about 14,000 characters, so a text over 13,500 opens Claude empty and stays on the clipboard to paste; the terminal link fills in at most 5,000, so Terminal does the same past 5,000. The app always asks before using a folder a link passes to Cowork or Code ("Folders passed through a link are treated as untrusted"), and the city cannot turn that off. The more menu also has Builder card in a Claude chat. Esc closes the panel before anything else.

### Claude Code at work (0.1.28)

A bubble over a building while Claude Code works in one of its folders, in the terminal, VS Code, JetBrains, or the Claude app's Code tab. Claude Code on the web (cloud sessions) does not read the user settings, so it shows nothing.
- **How it works:** hooks in Claude Code's user settings (`~/.claude/settings.json`, read by every local Claude Code) run `nullovation-city-status.py` at set moments. It writes one JSON file per session into a folder; the city reads that folder every 3 seconds while its tab shows (File System Access, Chrome and Edge). Until 0.1.35 the city only read it; since then it also writes there, for Claude to read and update the city (below).
- **The bubbles:** beside the urgency bubble (or the Tasks count), the pair centred over the building.
  - Working: coral, with Claude's spark turning between two frames.
  - Waiting for you (a permission, a question, or an MCP form): coral with a ?.
  - Finished, your turn: green with a tick. It shows until you open the building's status card, or for 12 hours.
  - Failed on an API error: grey lavender with a cross, the same way.
  - Several sessions on one building show their count; the most pressing state wins: waiting, working, failed, finished.
  - No bubbles hides them, and so does the postcard; Motion on Still holds the spark still. A click on one selects its building, like the others.
- **How long:** working goes quiet after 20 minutes without an event (an interrupt with Esc tells the hooks nothing), waiting after 8 hours, and idle sessions show nothing. An ended session shows nothing either, unless it finished and then closed (its last reply is kept, since a new prompt clears it): then it keeps its tick until you look, like any finish (0.1.29).
- **The status card:** since 0.1.59 the building's leader says it, or Proxima for a building with no leader, one line a session (The leaders' news, above): "Claude is working on <task>. Started 12 minutes ago.", waiting, finished, or stopped on an error, and for a finish the start of Claude's reply. Until then a line under the urgency said "Claude Code is working: <task>. Started 12 minutes ago." The task is the title of the copy from the city that started the session, else the first line of the latest prompt. Opening the card counts as seeing a finish, kept in this browser. Since 0.1.68 an update that reports on a finish shows under it as one report, and Tick it there comes only from Claude asking to tick the task off (Claude's reports, above).
  - A turn Claude Code starts by itself, such as a background task's notice, arrives as a prompt in a tag (`<task-notification>`); since 0.1.34 the city leaves it out of the card, and the hook keeps it out of `ask`, `project` and `task` while still marking the session working.
- **Tick it (0.1.34):** when a session started from a city copy finishes, the finish line offers Tick it for the task the copy named, while it is still open in the copy's project (found by title, any case). One click ticks it as a tick does: done, work for urgency, "Done: <task>" in Activity, and the toast "Ticked off: <task>.", which since 0.1.63 the building's leader says, with its face at 40 px, its name and the building, as the one-line add's toast (Open in Claude Panel D1); so is a repeat's toast, "<task> comes back <day>." with Undo. A building with no leader keeps the plain toast. With a session still working on the building, which takes the line, each finished session's task is named, "Finished: <task>", beside its own Tick it. A copy from another project, or a task renamed or done, offers nothing.
- **A line in Activity (0.1.37 to 0.1.68):** since 0.1.69 a finish writes nothing in Activity (Leader bubbles, above). Until then it left "Claude Code finished: <task>" in its project's Activity (the copy's task, else the prompt's first line; "Claude Code finished" alone with neither), at the time it finished, in time order, once per session and task: the entry's `ref` is `claude:<session>:<task>`. It makes the building busier for the week, as Activity does, but does not touch Updated or urgency: only a tick counts as work. Only the tab that writes the data file writes it.
- **The tab's title (0.1.37):** "(2) Nullovation City" counts the sessions waiting for you, the finishes and errors not yet seen, and Claude's changes that ask first; with none, plain "Nullovation City".
- **Which building:** the project whose folder link holds the session's working folder, the deepest when several do, in any case or slash, and from WSL (`/mnt/c/...`) or Git Bash (`/c/...`); a file link counts by its folder. Failing that, the project named on the "Project:" line of a copy from the city. A session no building claims, closed ones included (0.1.29), is listed in Settings, Links, to link its folder.
- **The setup:** `nullovation-claude-status.py`, from Settings, Links, Get the setup, embedded in the tool like the folder links setup (also `src/setup/` in the repo). Run once: a double-click, when .py files open with Python. With no arguments it asks which folder Claude Code should write to (since 0.1.30), installs, and waits for Enter before its window closes (`--no-pause` skips that); `--help` shows the usage.
  - The folder (0.1.30): a double-click opens a folder picker (tkinter; without it, a typed path, Enter keeping the shown one), starting at the folder already set, else `~/Nullovation City/Claude`. Cancel changes nothing. Running it again moves the hooks to the folder picked. `--install --folder PATH` names it without asking, and `--install` alone keeps the folder already set. Chrome cannot open folders inside AppData.
  - It copies itself to `~/.claude/hooks/nullovation-city-status.py`, makes the folder, leaves `README-nullovation-city.txt` in it saying what it is for (0.1.30), and on Windows puts the folder's path on the clipboard for the city's picker.
  - It adds 10 hooks: SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit and PostToolUse (working), PreToolUse on AskUserQuestion, PermissionRequest, and Notification on permission_prompt or elicitation_dialog (waiting), Notification on idle_prompt (idle, ending work that never reported its end), Stop (done), StopFailure (failed), SessionEnd (ended).
  - Each runs Python with no shell (`command` is the Python that ran the install, `args` the script, `hook`, the state and the folder), in the background (`async`), so it never slows Claude down. It prints nothing, so Claude reads nothing from it, and never blocks or changes anything.
  - It keeps every other setting and hook, saves the old file as `settings.json.nullovation-backup`, replaces its own hooks rather than doubling them, and refuses a settings file that is not valid JSON. `--uninstall` takes its hooks out; `--status` lists the sessions.
- **A session file** (`<session id>.json`): `app` and `kind` (`claude-session`), `session`, `state` (working, waiting, done, failed, idle, ended), `event`, `cwd`, `project` and `task` (from a copy), `ask` (the latest prompt's first line), `reply` (the start of the last reply), `entry` (Claude Code's entry point, kept but not shown), and `at`, `since` and `started` in milliseconds. A hook never overwrites a later event, swaps the file in whole, and notes failures in `hook-errors.log` in the folder. A new session clears files older than 3 days.
- **Settings, Links, Claude Code at work:** Connect the Claude folder; then the folder, when Claude Code was last heard from and what is working or waiting now, sessions no building claims, and Stop. Watching an empty folder with no README from the setup, it warns that Claude Code does not write there (0.1.30): the hooks and the city must name the same folder, and until 0.1.30 the setup picked its own without asking. Paused (the browser holding the folder back after a restart) or a missing folder shows under the logo too, with Allow them or Pick it again. Reset to defaults leaves the connection alone.

### Claude reads and updates the city (0.1.35, 0.1.36)

Both work through the Claude folder, which the city may now write in.
- **Write access (0.1.35):** connecting the Claude folder asks for read and write. A folder connected before 0.1.35 keeps its bubbles and asks once with Let it write, in Settings, Links and under the logo ("Claude cannot read the city until it may write in <folder>").
- **The summary (0.1.35):** `city/summary.json` in the Claude folder: `{ app, kind: 'city-summary', version: 1, written, about, leaders: [{ name, role, projects }], projects: [{ name, description, deadline, folders, leader: { name, role }, urgency: { system, state, reasons }, next, milestones: [{ name, open: [{ task, description, due, repeat }], waiting: [{ task, repeat, back }], done: [] }], notes: [{ title, text, written }], links: [{ label, url }] }] }`. No art, so about a tenth of the data file. Since 0.1.42 an open task that repeats has `repeat` in words, and a milestone with ticked repeats waiting lists them under `waiting`, with `back`, the day each comes back; both appear only when there is something to say. Since 0.1.49 the archive stays out: no archived task in `done`, and no milestone that went to the archive. Since 0.1.51 `leaders` names each leader with the projects it leads, and a project's `leader` is null without one. Since 0.1.53 an open task with a shift has `shift`, in words. Since 0.1.55 an open task with a wizard round has `round`: `{ title, kind, file, state }`, `kind` (0.1.63) `round` or `brainstorm`, the file as `wizard-rounds/<file>` and the state in words. Rewritten 1.5 seconds after a change, by the tab that writes the data file; Settings says when it was last written.
- **The config file (0.1.35):** the setup writes `~/.claude/nullovation-city.json`, `{ app, kind: 'claude-folder', folder, summary, inbox }`, so the nullovation-city skill finds the city's folder from any folder; `--uninstall` removes it. The setup has to run again once to write it.
- **The inbox (0.1.36):** Claude writes a change file into `inbox/` in the Claude folder; the tab that writes the data file picks it up within 3 seconds, in name order.
  - A change file: `{ app: 'nullovation-city', kind: 'change', version: 1, project, summary, changes: [{ do, ... }] }`. `project` is the project's name, any case. The kinds: `add-tasks` (`milestone`, `tasks: [{ text, description, due }]`), `add-note` (`title`, `text`), `add-link` (`label`, `url`), `done` (`task`), `edit-description` (`task`, `description`), `replace-plan` (`plan`, as a plan file's project), and since 0.1.55 `add-round` (`task`, and `file`, a round's file in `wizard-rounds`, or `round`, the round itself; since 0.1.63 a brainstorm too).
  - Applies at once, with a toast and Undo: add-tasks (into the milestone named, a new one by that name if none has it, else the current one), add-note, add-link (not one the project already has, by address), add-round (0.1.55: the task's round, replacing any it had; Activity logs "Claude attached a wizard round to <task>: <title>"). Since 0.1.59 its building's news says it, in place of the toast "Claude added 2 tasks, a note and 1 link in Garden planner.": "Claude added 2 tasks, a note and 1 link.", and each round "Claude attached a wizard round to <task>: <round>.", each with Undo. Since 0.1.68 the update is one report, under the finish of its task when its session finishes, with Read beside Undo and what was added named (Claude's reports, above). Undo takes back exactly what was added. Activity logged "Claude: <summary>" and "Claude wrote a note: <title>" until 0.1.68; since 0.1.69 only the round's line.
  - Asks first: done, edit-description, replace-plan. Done on a repeat, once applied, moves it on to its next date (0.1.42), and the card says "Tick off: X, on to its next date". They are written to `inbox/pending/`; the building's Claude bubble shows a ? (counting them with any session waiting), and its status card shows "Claude wants your OK first: <change>. Sent just now." in its leader's voice (0.1.59; "Claude asks first" until then; Claude's summary in the change's place until 0.1.67), and its news opens by itself with the same. Since 0.1.68 each change has its own short line and buttons, applied or dismissed on its own, and under a finish they are part of its report (Claude's reports, above); until then the changes were listed, a description cut at 140 characters, with one Apply and one Dismiss for all.
  - The file then moves to `inbox/done/`, so nothing applies twice. A file it cannot read, or a project the city does not have, moves to `inbox/rejected/` with an error toast. Done and rejected files are cleared after 14 days, checked once a day.
  - `69-inbox.js` (`ClaudeInbox`) does it; the nullovation-city skill writes the files (section 5).

### The city on a phone (0.1.74 to 0.1.76)

From Abdurrahman's task "Improve the phone experience" in Nullovation City Hall, UX Mastery: an analysis of the city on a phone, written up as `docs/phone-report.md` (what is bad, what is missing, what feels clunky), and its first part, what was plainly broken. The rest waits for his call (section 7).
- **Small screens:** a window under 760 px wide, as before, and since 0.1.74 a phone on its side too (under 520 px high, with a touch screen), get the drawer for the side bar, so the map has the whole width. `NARROW_SCREEN` and `narrowScreen()` in `00-util.js` say the same as the stylesheet.
- **The map's bar:** on a small screen its buttons wear their icons only, with less room between them, so the six fit 360 px; at 390 px it had run off the left edge, Traffic and Bubbles out of reach. The words stay for screen readers (`.mt-label`), and the drop-ups keep theirs.
- **Toasts** sit 70 px up on a small screen, over the bar.
- **The project view's top** fits a phone: under 780 px its columns are `minmax(0, 1fr)`, since a plain `1fr` kept the picture's 440 px and pushed the picture, the leader card and the About off the right edge.
- **The Menu button covers nothing:** the speech bubbles of Gus, Proxima and the leaders open under it on a small screen, 12 px in from the left and 58 px down, at most 400 px wide, with no arrow; and the status card keeps its top under it (`topRoom` in `50-bubble.js`).
- **The drawer covers the speech bubbles** and the panels on the map (z-index 36), so a bubble no longer covers Reports, Settings or the Leaders panel in the drawer.
- **A double tap steps inside:** a second touch within 350 ms and 30 px of the first counts as a double tap (`tapCount` in `40-map.js`), as a phone counts one, and does what a double-click does. A card opened by a tap lets taps through for those 350 ms (`Bubble.letThrough`), so the second tap reaches the building under it.
- **The shortcut wheel stays on the screen:** one wider than the map stands on a smaller ring, each label cut short only where it would leave the room either side of the middle, so those at the sides are cut and those at the top and the bottom keep theirs (`R_SMALL` in `64-wheel.js`; the ring's dashed circle follows `--r`).
- **Fields are 16 px on a touch screen** (`pointer: coarse`), so an iPhone does not zoom the page in when one is tapped; a project's name and a task's or a note's title keep their bigger size.
- **No words for keys a phone has not got:** on a touch screen the one-line add hides its line about Enter, Shift+Enter and Ctrl+Enter, and the panels' notes their words on Esc (`kbOnly` in `00-util.js`, the `kb-only` class), which stay in the page for a keyboard.
- **Room for a bubble:** a speech bubble that opens by itself (a leader's news, Proxima's report or party) waits while the project view or All tasks leaves less than 200 px of map beside it (`sayRoom` in `60-fullview.js`), as on a phone, where it covered the map and the bubble opened out of sight; it opens once the panel closes.
- **0.1.75, from The City on a Phone, Round 1 (section 6, The phone experience):**
  - **A long press opens the wheel (G1):** on a touch screen, a finger held still on a building for 500 ms opens its shortcut wheel, as a right-click does (`holdWheel`, `LONG_MS` in `40-map.js`); a phone's own long press, the `contextmenu` Android sends, says the same and opens no second wheel. Lifting the finger leaves the wheel open and clicks nothing under it. On a touch screen nothing lifts a building any more: buildings move in Edit City, with a plain drag. A mouse keeps hold then drag, after 380 ms. The map takes no callout or text selection from a long press.
  - **Back (G2):** while anything is open on a touch screen (the one-line add, a drop-up, Open in Claude's panel, the wheel, a ghost's card, a dialog or a panel, a leader's editor, the project view, planting or moving, Edit City, a status card, All tasks, Settings, Reports, the Leaders panel, the drawer), one entry in the browser's history stands guard. Android's back gesture takes it away; the city closes one layer, as Esc does, but keeping what was typed, as Done does (`FullView.back`: a section being edited, a task, a note or a milestone kept; a task's note back to its task, an archived task to the archive), and stands guard again while more is open. Closed another way, the last layer takes its guard with it, so on the bare map back leaves the city, as before. A computer's back button is left alone. In `90-boot.js`, beside Esc.
  - **Bigger areas, the same look (B2):** on a touch screen (`pointer: coarse`) icon buttons answer taps 6 px round them, small buttons 6 px above and below, and checkboxes 9 px above and below and 10 px to the left but only 3 px to the right, short of the task's name; a news face's dismiss reaches 8 px up and left, away from its face. Each is an invisible `::after`, so nothing moves.
  - **Copy only (B4):** Open in Claude's panel on a touch screen (`touchScreen()` in `00-util.js`, no mouse to point) is a wide Copy row, under a task's Build it and Think, with no Chat, Cowork, Code, Terminal or folder line.
  - **The bubbles' face (L2):** 64 px on a small screen (`sayFace()` for a leader's, which is drawn at its size; `--face` for Gus's and Proxima's), and `fitSay` squeezes a bubble by its face, so the words stay beside it on a phone.
  - **The phone's guide (S1):** Settings' ? holds two lists, the keys (`.help-keys`) and a phone's (`.help-touch`), and a touch screen shows the second.
  - **The zoom's number (S2):** on a touch screen, `cam.z`: the screen pixels a building pixel takes.
  - **Left as they were:** no undo in the task panel on a phone (G3: Done closes it, keeping what was typed); a news face's dismiss on its corner (B3); the shift tags under every untagged row in All tasks (L1, "leave it, this panel will need a rework soon anyway"). Where a phone opens the one-line add (B1) went to Round 2, with how a phone adds a task at all.
- **0.1.76, from The City on a Phone, Round 2 (section 6, The phone experience): adding a task on a phone.**
  - **+ Task (A2):** right after + New project in the side bar, a plain button beside the pink one, on the PC too, its tooltip "Add a task (T)". It opens the line over the map (`QuickAdd.open`), on the project open in the view or the selected building, else the project last added to, as Enter and T do. On a phone it is Menu, then + Task: the drawer closes for the line, and the focus goes back to Menu after it. While a new project is named, both buttons step aside for the name (`#newBtns` in `70-menu.js`).
  - **The mark keys (A1, A1a):** on a touch screen only, four keys under the line, Project, Milestone, Date and Repeat, each in its mark's color (pink, sky blue, gold, peach), 30 px tall, as the shift tags under them are on a phone. A tap writes the mark's sign at the end of the line and opens its list, the focus kept in the line so the keyboard stays up; a pick writes the whole mark, in its color, as typing does (`keyMark` in `66-quickadd.js`). With that kind of mark in the line already, a key opens its whole list over it, the caret at its end and the one there lit, and a pick writes over it, both words of a date such as "!nov 20". A sign a key wrote goes again when its list closes with nothing picked or typed after it, so no stray sign lands in a title (`dropBare`); typed after, it is a mark like any. Milestone with no project in the line says "Pick a project with / first". The line's placeholder is "A task" there. A computer shows no keys: a mark is one key on a keyboard.
  - **Pick a date (A4):** on a touch screen the date list ends with Pick a date ("The calendar"). The phone's own date field lies over its row, unseen, at 16 px and from today on, so a tap opens the phone's calendar; the day picked is written over the date mark as "!nov 20", or "!2027-11-20" when the month and day alone would read as another year (`dateMark`).
  - **The list under the keys:** on a phone the list opens under the keys rather than over them, so a key can be tapped with a list open, and at 284 px it shows the week and Pick a date with no scrolling. Back closes the list before the line, as Esc does (`QuickAdd.back`).
  - **The card's Add a task went (A3),** on the PC as on a phone, in his words: "actually it is time. I never use this and this card is still one of the most annoying cards in the app. remove it, even in PC. you don't add tasks from here."
  - **The phone's guide** says "+ Task, in the menu; the keys under its line pick the project, milestone, date and repeat".
- **Data:** nothing new in the city or the browser.

### The app (0.1.67)

The first of the two Quick Sync builds (section 6, Quick Sync, O1), from Abdurrahman's task "Make the city an installable app" in Nullovation City Hall, Quick Wins. Sync comes after it. `17-app.js` (`AppInstall`) holds it; `src/app/` the manifest, the icons and the worker's source.
- **What it is:** the hosted city installs like the Decision Wizard: its own window and icon, from the PC or the phone. It opens offline from a copy it keeps, and online the newest version always loads.
- **The worker:** `sw.js`, built from `src/app/sw.js` beside the page, since a service worker only looks after the pages in its own folder and GitHub Pages cannot widen that; each version changes the file, so the browser takes the new worker. It keeps the page and the app's files (the manifest and four icons) in a cache of its own, `nullovation-city-app`, since the wizard's app shares the address. The page goes to the network first, asking the server each time (a quick "not changed" when it has not), and the copy answers only when the network fails or errs. Every other request passes by untouched.
- **The manifest:** `src/app/manifest.webmanifest`: Nullovation City, short name City, standalone, its start and scope the page's own folder (`../../` from `src/app/`), focusing a window already open rather than opening another, the title bar in the side bar's dark (#1A1628) and the window opening on the page's own background (#120F1F). The page names it, with the iPhone's icon, only when it is hosted (https, or localhost for the tests): a page opened from a file cannot fetch it and would say so in the console. The page itself carries `<meta name="theme-color">`.
- **The icon (picked 24 October 2026, C of three):** the city hall's glass dome with the tiny city inside, on night violet (#3A2D60), drawn from `src/buildings/city-hall.png` by `art/app/icons.py`, which cuts the dome at the foot of its pink rim, keeps only the piece joined to its middle, and closes the rim's ends in the hall's outline color. A at 2x (the whole city hall on its plot, haze blue) blurred into a blob at 32 px; B (the side bar's skyline logo, blush pink) read as a bar chart. `icon-512.png` and `icon-192.png` are rounded squares with see-through corners, `icon-maskable-512.png` fills its square for Android to cut, and `icon-180.png` is solid for an iPhone's home screen, which rounds it itself. The tab's own favicon is unchanged.
- **Settings, Saving, The app:** in the hosted page, Install the app when the browser offers it (`beforeinstallprompt`, kept for the button), with "Its own window and icon. It opens offline, and loads the newest version whenever you're online. Installed from this browser, it opens this same city."; a no keeps the button, a yes takes it away, and once installed the toast says "Installed. The city opens from your apps now, offline too." With no offer (installed already, or a browser that has none, such as Safari): the browser's menu may offer Install app, or Add to Home screen on a phone. Inside the app: "You're in the installed app." A local copy opened from a file says it cannot be installed and links the hosted city.
- **Which city it opens:** installed from a Chromium browser, the app shares that browser's storage for the address, so it opens that browser's city, with its data file and backups folder, which may ask to be allowed again in the app's window. Installed from another browser, it starts a new city, moved in with a backup. On the phone it is a city of its own until sync lands, kept in the browser only: the data file, daily backups and the Claude folder need Chrome or Edge on a computer. An iPhone keeps a home screen app's storage apart from Safari's.
- **Data:** nothing new in the city or the browser's settings; the worker's cache holds the page and the app's files.

### Sync (0.1.73)

Part 1 of the two sync builds (section 6, Quick Sync; the plan is `docs/quick-sync-plan.md`): every city that syncs keeps the same city through one shared copy in a private GitHub repository, until production's database. `18-sync.js` (`Sync`) holds it.
- **Setting it up, once:** a private repository on github.com with a README (Add a README ticked), and a fine-grained token for each device, for that repository only, with Contents: Read and write. Then Settings, Saving, Sync, under The app: the repository (`AbdurrahmanKh/nullovation-city-data` filled in) and the token, then Connect. How to set it up unfolds the three steps.
- **Connect's checks,** before anything is written, each with its own line:
  - a token GitHub refuses: "GitHub refused the token. Check it was copied whole.";
  - a repository the token does not reach: "GitHub finds no <repo> for this token. ...";
  - a public repository: "<repo> is public. The city holds every project, note, link and folder path, so sync needs a private repository.";
  - an empty one: "<repo> is empty. On GitHub, add a README to it, then connect again.";
  - a token that can only read: "The token can only read. Give it Contents: Read and write, then paste it again."
  - The write check makes an empty blob, which no commit names.
- **The first city starts the shared copy:** with no `city.json` in the repository, this city writes it whole and rewrites the README to say what the repository is. Its toast: "Synced. This city started the shared copy in <repo>."
- **Any other city takes it:** it asks first, "Take the shared city?", saying how many projects the copy has and that a backup downloads first. Then `nullovation-city-backup-before-sync-YYYY-MM-DD.json` downloads, and the city becomes the shared one, pictures and all.
- **The shared copy:**
  - `city.json`, `{ app, kind: 'shared-city', format: 1, data: 3, wrote, at, world, city, leaders, order }`: the map, the city's settings, the leaders with their portraits named by fingerprint (`portraitFile`), the projects' order, the version that wrote it, and the time of the sending city's last change. Every send writes it.
  - `projects/<id>.json`: one per project, with its picture named by fingerprint (`art.file`).
  - `art/<sha256>.<ext>`: each picture once, as its own bytes.
  - Files are written with their keys sorted, so the same thing written in another order reads alike and sends nothing.
- **Checking:** the tab that syncs asks GitHub for the branch's latest commit every 15 seconds while in view, and on opening, coming back into view and coming online. It names the commit it has, so "nothing new" is quick and free, and asks with the browser's cache off.
- **Sending:**
  - 3 seconds after the last change, and at once on going out of view, it sends one commit on top of its base: the projects that changed, any new picture and `city.json`. Then it moves the branch to that commit.
  - GitHub refuses the move when another city wrote since; the city then reads the new copy, merges and sends again.
  - A read that lags behind a send is passed over.
  - The commit names what changed, such as "City 0.1.73: Garden planner; 1 picture".
- **The base:** the shared copy as this city last synced it, with its commit, kept in IndexedDB's new `sync` store (IndexedDB version 3).
- **The merge (C1):** three ways, thing by thing: a project's own fields, each task, milestone, note and link, each leader, each city setting, the map and the projects' order.
  - A task, milestone, note, link or leader goes field by field, so a title changed in one city and a description in the other both stay; a task's own notes merge as a list of their own.
  - A change on one side takes that side's version.
  - A clash, the same field changed on both sides, goes to the newer side: this city's last change, kept with the token, against `city.json`'s `at`. A change not yet saved is saved first, so it is weighed with its own time.
  - A thing deleted on one side goes, unless the other side changed it and is the newer.
  - An order comes from the side that changed it, and what is new goes after what its side had before it.
  - Activity keeps both sides' lines, less those either side took out with an Undo.
  - Updated and the last work keep the later, and the city keeps one star, the newer side's.
  - Then the city reads the result as a backup is read: plots that clash, and pins and leaders that point at nothing.
- **In place:** a city received takes effect with no reload.
  - Every project, task, milestone, note, link and leader still there stays the same object, so what is open stays open and keeps working, and the project view redraws.
