  - **S2, approved:** a crowd of about 20 dances round the block; six party drones loop over it in the colors of its particles; a bus pulls up to watch; its particles all party long; party lights over its streets; the announcement with Go to it, with his note "It gets announced by proxima pace", so it is her speech bubble rather than a toast. Left out: a banner with the milestone's name.
  - **Round 2, A1, Proxima's lines for it:** approved, "Milestone landed! {milestone}: {n} tasks done in {project}. Street party's on!" and "Copy that, {milestone} is logged: {n} for {n} in {project}. The block's dancing!"; left out, "{n} tasks, {milestone} complete! {project} is throwing a street party. Next on the flight plan: {next}." and "Touchdown! {project} landed {milestone}, all {n} tasks. Next up: {next}."
  - **Reasoned in the rounds, not asked:** the burst plays like the wizard's; particles only, no cheer from the people; particles at the map's scale with the dark outline, keeping their colors at night; a tick anywhere counts, unticking plays nothing, and Still shows none of it; the pick lives in the Building panel, a tile per kind playing its burst when pointed at, and travels with the city; a milestone throws its party once a day; with the traffic off only the particles and lights play; it shows on the map and in the project view's picture.
  - **Reasoned while building:** the burst starts 0.42 of the way down the building; a party's own bursts start 2 seconds after the tick's; the lights are three strings across each of the four streets, sorted by depth with the figures; the dance is the talking arms and a new arms-up frame, with a hop; guests come across the block's zebras and out of its front, and walk off across the first zebra afterwards, or into the building after 6 seconds without one; anyone walking on to the block joins in; the bus takes the shortest way to a street with the block on its right, at 1.6 tiles a second, skipping its stops, and drives on a second after the party ends; the party drones fly in high from the map's edge and loop at 34 to 79 px; picking particles is not work, like a pin; Go to it glides the building to the middle of the open map, and her bubble sits beside the project view while it is open; she announces on Still too; a kind the tool does not have reads as stars.

- **Ghost tasks (19 and 20 October 2026, Ghost Tasks, Round 1, `2026-10-19-ghost-tasks-round1.json`, delivered in the chat and answered the same day). Built at 0.1.47; section 2, Ghost tasks.** The details of C1 to C4 of Tasks, Round 2.
  - **G1, "building with urgency":** behind its pace: one or more periods behind under One task per N days, or a day without a ticked task under Work until finished, the same buildings that make garbage through urgency. Not picked: any red or yellow bubble, or red bubbles only.
  - **G2:** at most 5 ghosts round one block at once.
  - **M1:** on the sidewalk, among the people, who walk through it. Not picked: in the street, or along its plot's rim.
  - **M2:** a ticked ghost rises and fades. Not picked: slipping into its building, or fading where it is.
  - **L1:** a sheet ghost; **L1a:** see-through inside its glowing edge. Not picked: a note ghost, a hologram ghost; a pale solid body.
  - **L2:** its eyes and edges glow in its building's bubble color, red or yellow. Not picked: ghost cyan for all.
  - **L3:** 12 px tall at 1x, in his words: "The ghosts will be clickable/or you point over them, and you see the task."
  - **S1:** its own switch in Settings, Look, on by default and kept on this computer; Traffic and No bubbles leave them. **S2:** hidden on Motion Still; shown on the postcard and in the project view's picture.
  - **X1:** now: a due chip on its card, its task on hover, peeking round corners; later: slipping through its building's wall; left out: Open in Claude on its card, and a faint trail. On Open in Claude he asked "what do you mean by on its card, what is the card?": the small card a click on a ghost opens (C4).
  - **Reasoned in the round, not asked:** a task both overdue and next is one ghost; the next task is the one the status card puts first; on rest days and away only overdue tasks haunt; a waiting repeat never haunts; it creeps at about half walking speed with a slight bob and pauses, lingers by its building's front, never crosses a street, and nothing reacts to it; it comes out of its building's front when its task starts to haunt and is already on its round when the city opens; one that stops haunting without a tick leaves as a ticked one does; Edit City hides them; the ghost is shown at 1x and 2x, by day and night, for his approval.
  - **Reasoned while building:** the next task is `bubbleTodos`' first, so a pinned task not overdue gets its own ghost beside the overdue ones; past 5, the most overdue come out first, then the next task; the eyes look the way it goes, which also makes the peek read; the body is white at 0.62, since a fainter one read as an empty arch by day; a faint dark ring by day and a soft glow ring at night; peeking is a stop at the corner, a lean of 0.13 tiles on to the new side with its eyes there, and back; the ghost holds still while its card is open, and its card closes the status card and the wheel, as they close each other; Enter steps inside with the task open, since the card is about the task; Tick has a toast with Undo, since the card goes; a click on a ghost never starts moving the building behind it.

- **Archiving (20 October 2026, Archiving, Round 1, `2026-10-20-archiving-round1.json`, delivered in the chat and answered the same day). Built at 0.1.49; section 2, The archive.** The details of A1 to A3 of Tasks, Round 2: an Archive view in the project's more menu, newest first, with Restore (A1); by hand and by itself, a done task archiving itself 7 days after its tick (A2, A2a); search and the reports, recap and review still see archived work, while a milestone's Copy for Claude and the city summary leave it out (A3).
  - **B1:** a milestone goes to the archive with them once every task in it is archived. Not picked: staying in the list, or folding shut by itself.
  - **B2:** a milestone's count and the project's progress still count archived tasks. Not picked: leaving them out.
  - **A1, by hand:** now, Archive in a done task's panel, an archive button on a done row, and Archive done tasks per milestone; left out, Archive all done per project.
  - **C1, Restore:** back as done, and it archives itself again 7 days later. Not picked: back as done and staying until archived by hand, or back as open.
  - **C2, deleting from the archive:** in his words: "I am not sure what is the 'archive view' right now, I cannot imagine it, but in my mind I feel like you can open the task, as archived task but you can see it. and then unarchive it from there.(restore) Over there, since it is the same current task view, it should have a delete." So an archived task opens in the same task panel, to read, with Restore and Delete task; the Archive view's rows keep Restore (A1) and have no Delete.
  - **C3, the Archive view's layout:** by milestone. Not picked: one list, or by week.
  - **D1, done tasks already in the city:** archived at once on the first open. Not picked: 7 days from the update, or leaving them.
  - **E1, signs of the archive:** a line at a milestone's foot, with his note "4 [archives icon] no text."; archived tasks in the milestone view; and the count in the more menu.
  - **Reasoned in the round, not asked:** only done tasks archive, so a repeat never does; the 7 days count from the tick's day and stay fixed, checked on opening and when the day changes; unticking before then keeps a task in its list; archiving writes nothing in Activity and touches neither Updated nor urgency; Reports, the streak, the recap and Proxima read ticks, so they count archived work as before; search still finds a building by its archived tasks; the weekly review A3 named was taken out at 0.1.44; the Archive view opens beside the project view, as a milestone does; Claude's done and Tick it act only on open tasks; export, backups and the data file carry archived tasks; the builder card counts them as done; a done task with no tick on record counts from the day of the update.
  - **Reasoned while building:** archived tasks stay in the project's todos, marked, so everything that counts them keeps working; a milestone is in the archive when it has tasks and every one is archived, worked out each time rather than stored, so a restore or a task added brings it back by itself; a plan loaded over a project keeps the archived tasks with their own milestones, which stay in the archive with them (the round had said their milestone's name, and a restore into the current milestone; keeping the milestone does that and lets B1 bring it back); a project export loaded as a plan leaves its archive out, so loading it back does not double it; deleting the last task of a milestone in the archive takes the milestone too, since nothing would show it again; the archive's rows say the day each was ticked; Esc on an archived task goes back to the archive, the layer it came from; a drag or Alt and an arrow fills the shown tasks' places in the new order, so every archived task keeps its place for Restore; the menu says "Archive" alone with nothing in it; restoring a task whose milestone is gone puts it where a new task would go.
  - **After his first look (20 October 2026), built at 0.1.50:** in his words: "The archive view, It is ok to have every milestone open, but you shoule be able to collapse them in the archive panel just like in the task list." Reasoned while building: the fold is the archive's own, apart from the list's, so folding one never hides the other; it is kept on the milestone and travels with the city, as the list's does; opening the archive at a milestone or a task unfolds it, so what you came for shows.

- **All tasks (20 October 2026, three rounds, `2026-10-20-all-tasks-round1.json` to `-round3.json`, delivered in the chat with mockups drawn in the tool on a made-up city, answered the same day, and the shifts' names asked in the chat). Built at 0.1.53; section 2, All tasks and shifts.** The details of L1 and L2 of Tasks, Round 2.
  - **Round 1, a list in the side bar:** A1, ways in: a button at the side bar's foot, L, and Due this week's "and N more" line; not the map's bar. A2, building tiles with their counts. B1, a row's project and milestone. B2, the starred task first, under Starred. C1, day groups: Overdue, Today, Tomorrow, Rest of this week, Next week, Later. C2, the undated tasks by building, those behind their pace first. C3, 3 each, then Show all. D1, now: a row lighting its building, the arrow keys with Enter and Space, a count line, a words field; left out: an add field, a date button on each row.
  - **Turned down, then kept:** he did not like the list mockup: "the all tasks should be a better idea, d-wiz this again, but now in mind ways to make the view better, more focused, things that help me organize better, IDK, if having them distributed in buildings better, I don't need this extra layer." Round 2 read that as tasks spread over the map (next tasks on the map, one at a time, tracked tasks), which was wrong: "I meant if the current task view (building-> project view) is good enough, there is no reason to create more; I did not mean to suggest that to actually show all tasks in the city! The same list you did, but with more functionality or a better view. if you have no ideas on how to make it useful we can skip it." So Round 1's list stands, with kinds of work now (K1).
  - **Round 3, the kinds and what they add:** in his words, "The types are not based on the kind of tasks based on what they are, they are based on the way and when I am able to achieve things": Create (code, art, test: a screen and a keyboard, maybe an AI agent running, and Claude might come back to ask; heavy work for work hours), Think (design, write, decide: sometimes a screen, but pen and paper will do), and a third for away from the screen (daily tasks, buying, calling someone). On decide: "it will just be a Think task, weather a d-wizard round is saved or not." And: "this task is simpler than you think." K3, one per task. K4, "You cannot add kinds for now." L1, now: the chips with counts, sorting the untagged in one click, and the counts per building: "In the list, it should show the number for each building, and the city itself doesn't care." Left out: opening on the last one picked, Copy per building, grouping by kind.
  - **The names (asked in the chat):** the feature is a task's Shift (over Mode, Context and Kind), and the third shift is Errand (over Handle, Go and Out; Away clashes with Settings' away until). No fourth (Talk and Wait not taken).
  - **Reasoned while building:** the marks match a shift by its start; the tag colors are the city's teal, lavender and lawn green; setting a shift is not work, like a pin; the panel takes the focus when it opens, not the field, so L closes it again; a ticked row keeps its place, date and group for its moment, a repeat too; the chips count within the building picked and the tiles within the shift picked, and the count line counts what the list shows; a tile or a row pointed at lights its building.

- **Tasks into notes (22 October 2026, Abdurrahman's task "Turn tasks into Ideas(Notes)" in Nullovation City Hall, Task Improvements). Built at 0.1.54; section 2, Tasks into notes, and back.**
  - **Asked for:** in his words: "Some tasks I'd like to keep as an open idea. I don't want them to be "tasks" no more. so you can click a task, and then Click [Turn into a note] you can also turn a note into a task. Also since links are main thing in my app, I want them to appear above notes (notes currently above them)".
  - **Reasoned while building, not asked:** a note holds a title and a text, so a task's title and description carry over and the rest goes, Undo bringing it all back; a note becomes a task where a task added without @ goes, the tool's one rule for a task with no milestone named, and its panel opens so where it went shows, to drag elsewhere if wanted; the buttons sit at each panel's foot left of Delete, as Archive done tasks sits by Delete milestone, and the note panel's foot is laid out the same way, since three things beside its written line crowded it; each panel closes as always before turning, so a tick or an edit made in it keeps its Activity line; a done task can be turned too, an archived one cannot until restored, being read-only; a title longer than a note's 200 characters opens the note's text rather than being cut; neither is work, and both move Updated, like adding a task or writing a note; the recap counts a note turned into a task as added, as it counts a task turned into a note among the notes written; Undo takes back the lines it wrote; links sit above notes in the same column, level with Todos.
  - **Found while building:** opening a note while a task's or a milestone's panel was open skipped closing that panel, so a tick or an edit made there wrote no line in Activity. Fixed at 0.1.54: the note panel closes whatever was open first, as the task and milestone panels already did.
- **Wizard rounds in a task (22 October 2026, three rounds, `2026-10-22-task-rounds-round1.json` to `-round3.json`, delivered in the chat, the third with three sheets of mockups drawn in the tool, answered the same day). Built at 0.1.55; section 2, Wizard rounds in a task.** The details of W1 to W6 of Tasks, Round 2. He brought the task asking: "I think I need to plan this further-decision wizard this task."
  - **Round 1, M1, Make a wizard round in Open in Claude's menu:** a second row under Chat, Cowork, Claude Code and Terminal, with the same four. Not picked: a switch, or one Terminal item.
  - **F1, the shared folder:** `wizard-rounds` inside the Claude folder. Not picked: a folder of its own.
  - **A1, when a round counts as answered,** in his words: "I need to make sure what the flow is. First, for now, it is ok to have it when nothing is open, but sometimes I skip a few questions on purpose. but I need to understand the full flow." So: when nothing is open, and Round 2 asked about skips.
  - **A2, answers in Copy task,** in his words: "I think it should be done through files. The task knows where the file is. Also, I want the task to have a gray hat if not answered and colored Hat when it is." So Copy task points to the round's file, and the hat stays on, gray, then colored, rather than coming off.
  - **X1, extras:** now, the hat in the hover's next-task line, the hat in What is next, a click on a row's hat doing Wizard it, a toast when answers arrive, and a hat on an answered round; left out, a Rounds chip in All tasks, rounds Claude writes on its own attaching to the task, and a hat beside a building's bubbles.
  - **Round 2, S1, a round with questions skipped on purpose:** Mark answered in the task. Not picked: the wizard marking it, or both.
  - **C1, a round's answers in a chat,** which cannot open a file on the PC: answers written in. Open in Claude's Chat writes them into the copy; every other copy names the file.
  - **Round 3, from mockups, P1, the round's place in the task panel:** a card of its own, under Pin and Star, before the description. **P1a:** folded. Not picked: one row, or with Copy task.
  - **P2, a task with no round:** a line only while a file is dragged over the panel. Not picked: a quiet line, or nothing.
  - **H1, the answered hat's color:** teal, with his note: "The gray one: the white lines make it look a bit active: let's make it look like a flat icon, not pixel art, just like the one in the wizard, no details, no multi-color. and the teal too. its ok to have smooth icons." So both hats are the wizard's own icon, flat and smooth in one color. Not picked: violet, pink.
  - **Reasoned in the rounds, not asked:** the flow. Make a wizard round sends the task's copy asking Claude for a round; Claude Code writes it into `wizard-rounds` and sends it by the inbox, while a chat gives a file to drop on the task; Wizard it opens the wizard on that folder, which saves the answers into the same file; the city reads them back and says so. With no Claude folder, Wizard it downloads the file and its answers file is dropped back.
  - **After his test (24 October 2026), built at 0.1.65:** in his words: "when I click wizard it, first of all it open in my browser not in the wizard app. I'd really love for it to be opened in the wizard app. and second it shows a very old round open, it doesn't show the wizard round." Reasoned while building: `window.open` gives the wizard an opener, and Chrome takes only links without one into an installed app, so Wizard it clicks a link instead, and the wizard's manifest asks for the window it has; the link carries the round itself, so the wizard opens it whatever it had open and whether or not it has the folder, which the answers still go through; it carries the answers so far too, which a wizard without the round takes and one with it leaves alone; the download with no Claude folder goes, since the round now arrives in the link. The wizard's side is wizard 0.11.1, in its own repo.
  - **After his second test (24 October 2026), built at 0.1.66:** in his words: "It did not work. I just tested it, I clicked the wizard it and the icon on the task, both opened a new page in the browser. I am using Maxthon; that could be the reason but I am not sure. it opened to the correct round though this time. but the app did not open". Found on his PC: the city runs in Maxthon 7.5 (Chromium 140), and the app his desktop and Start menu open is installed from Chrome. Tested with throwaway profiles: Chrome takes every kind of link into its app (a real link, a script's, `window.open` with noopener) once the app opens in its own window, but only links clicked in Chrome; Maxthon takes none, even into a wizard installed in Maxthon with link capturing on; and Chrome registers an app's `protocol_handlers` with Windows, so a `web+dwizard:` link Windows opens launches the app on the round it names. Reasoned while building: the app route goes through `web+dwizard:`, naming the round since Windows takes about 2,000 characters, and the app reads the file from the Claude folder; it needs the Claude folder, and the hosted wizard, since the app is the hosted one; the tab route stays for Browser tab, an address of your own and no Claude folder; the toast always offers Open in a tab, for a computer without the app or before the app knows the link.
  - **Reasoned while building (0.1.55):** the wizard opens in a tab of its own, the same one each time, from the click itself so the browser allows it; Wizard it writes the file again when it is gone and leaves one already there alone; the first Wizard it says to give the wizard the folder once; answers count only when the wizard names the round, or names nothing, and a toast comes 4 seconds after the last one lands; one tab reads them, the one that writes the data file; the same round dropped again updates it in place, another replaces it; dropping, answering, marking and removing are not work; Esc in the task panel keeps the round, which comes from outside; a ticked, waiting or archived task keeps its round and wears no hat, and an archived task's card is to read; the hat in All tasks sits at the start of a row's details; Settings, Links has the wizard's address, the hosted wizard when empty; Cowork gets the round's file when its full path is known; a round past 300,000 characters is not kept.

No: picked marks as chips, with his note "Just make the special commands have unique colors"; pasting several lines.

Left out of the round on purpose, as settled in Tasks, Round 2 (O1): ^ for pin, * for star, and #repeat alone.

- **Quick Sync (23 and 24 October 2026, Abdurrahman's task "Quick Sync plan" in Nullovation City Hall, Quick Wins: "I need to plan the best way to have PWA app like we did with the wizard, but sync the data between the cities. withoiut the DB and the file storage planned for prodction." Quick Sync, Round 1, `2026-10-23-quick-sync-round1.json`, written in a chat, which also planned it in the claude.ai project's `claude/quick-sync.md`, and answered 24 October; Round 2, `2026-10-24-quick-sync-round2.json`, attached to the task; both in the Claude folder's `wizard-rounds`). The app built at 0.1.67, and sync's part 1 at 0.1.73; section 2, The app and Sync.**
  - **S1, where the shared copy lives:** a private GitHub repository (27 October). His note, "a private github means I need to commit a file from my phone, seem strange.", read the repository as committing by hand; on both GitHub routes the city writes the file itself through GitHub's API with a token pasted once in each city. Round 2 asked again between a private repository (recommended) and a secret gist; a cloud folder and a computer he runs were not taken up. Round 2 came back the same day as a question, in his words: "how would a city push to git? will the software have git. I don't understand and please consider other ways, don't d-wiz the next, I am not making a decision I want to understand". Answered in the chat: no git, the page writes through GitHub's web API and GitHub makes the commit; the other ways laid out beside it (Dropbox with a sign-in, Google Drive and OneDrive, whose sign-ins in a web page last an hour and a day, a small server of his own on a free host, and Round 1's two). He picks in the chat, not in a round. Picked on 27 October: a private repository, in his words: "Create a plan for synching with github private repo (although I believe this is a bad way to do it but you did not provide good options for me, maybe that is the way until production)". The plan is `docs/quick-sync-plan.md`.
  - **S2:** an open city checks every 15 seconds while in view, and when it opens or comes back into view; it sends 3 seconds after the last change.
  - **J1:** the installed app on the PC, the local copy he tests builds with, the phone and a second computer sync; a borrowed browser stays out. On the second computer, in his words: "in the city I enter the token, so I can choose to sync it from anywhere." The token stays in its browser, never in the data file, a backup or an export.
  - **C1:** the newest change wins, with no question: "I only picked this because it is the simplest implementation, the backups will solve problems if needed."
  - **R1:** reports read, what Gus and Proxima said today and the milestone parties thrown today travel; Claude Code at work stays in each city ("For now, but for later, I want to be able to work from my phone"), and so do Claude's changes that ask first.
  - **M1:** a change made in another city plays as if done here: the burst, the party, the truck and the ghost rising away.
  - **O1:** the app first, then sync. The app is built at 0.1.67 (section 2, The app), with the icon he picked on 24 October from three drawn from the city's own art: C, the city hall's dome.
  - **Reasoned, not asked:** sw.js is built to the repo root beside `index.html`, since a service worker only controls pages in its own folder and GitHub Pages cannot widen that, and the manifest and icons live in `src/app/`; the icons are art, shown for approval first; the app shares `abdurrahmankh.github.io` with the wizard's app, and with it that address's storage, so clearing one app's site data clears the other's; until sync lands the app is a separate city, moved in with a backup and kept off the local copy's data file; the first city to connect starts the shared copy, any other saves a backup of itself and then takes it; a send merges against the copy the city last synced, thing by thing (a project's fields, each task, milestone, note, link, leader and city setting), the newer change winning a clash and Activity lines from both kept; each picture goes once, named by its fingerprint; the leaders' news heard travels with Gus's and Proxima's; on the PC one city at a time does the PC's jobs (the data file, daily backups, the summary and Claude's inbox), as one tab does today; a city that cannot read the shared copy's version stops sending and asks for a reload; sync trouble shows under the logo, like saving; the tests never sync, since their fresh browsers hold no token.
  - **Measured, 24 October:** the data file is 2.2 MB, of which 1.8 MB is building art (1.6 MB) and portraits (0.2 MB), so the city without pictures is about 0.4 MB.
  - **Reasoned while building the app (0.1.67):** the title bar is the side bar's dark (#1A1628), since the tool's look is always dark, and the window opens on the page's own background; the page names its manifest only when hosted, since a page opened from a file cannot fetch it and the tests open the page from a file; the worker handles only the page and the app's files and keeps its own cache, the wizard's app sharing the address; the page asks the server each time, so a push shows at the next open; Install sits first in Settings, Saving, its note saying the app opens this same city only where that is true (a Chromium browser's offer), not under the menu's hint, since an iPhone keeps a home screen app apart; the module is `AppInstall`, since `App` is the selection state.
  - **Reasoned while building sync, part 1 (0.1.73):**
    - `city.json` is written with every send, its `at` the sending city's last change, so a clash compares the two cities' own times. It also carries the projects' order (`order`), which a folder of files does not keep.
    - The version guard reads `wrote`, the version that wrote the copy, as well as the data version. An older city would drop what a newer one added and send it back without it, so a copy from a newer version stops sending until a reload. It also lifts by itself once a later commit can be read.
    - Taking the shared copy asks first, as Import does, since it replaces the city.
    - One tab syncs, with its own claim (`nullovation-city:syncWriter`), renewed with each check, so a tab left open takes over 45 seconds after the syncing one goes quiet. The data file's writer is claimed apart, as before.
    - A city received keeps every object still there, since a panel's fields hold the task they edit.
    - Tasks, milestones, notes, links and leaders merge field by field, finer than the plan's thing by thing, so two cities editing one task at once lose neither edit; found while testing, when a description written on the phone just after a title changed on the PC was lost. For the same reason a change not yet saved is saved before the city weighs its time against the shared copy's.
    - Connect's write check makes an empty blob, which nothing names.
    - The pass is test46_sync, since test44 went to Leader Bubbles.

Let's start simple." Round 1, `2026-10-24-claude-news-card-round1.json` in the Claude folder's `wizard-rounds`, attached to the task and answered the same day). A1, A2, B3 and C2 built at 0.1.68; section 2, Claude's reports.**
  - **A1:** one report. Comment: "I need to review what shows in the bubble and what doesn't I think most items with actions should be bubbled."

Also from B1: "we might need a way to organize notes now, Like: Claude will add to its own notebook." Explained in the chat, to pick there (no round).

  - **Reasoned while building:** "most items with actions should be bubbled" read as: a report opens by itself while it holds something to act on, as additions and asks did, and a finish with nothing to act on still waits with its leader (Leader UX, M1); an update from a session still at work waits for its finish, so the report opens once and whole; an update with no finish to sit under is said on its own, as before (the round's A1 note); the update finds its session by the task it names, else by the one session working on the building or that just finished there; Read is a small panel over the city, and until B1 is decided it shows a new description whole over the one it would replace; each change that asks first is applied or dismissed on its own line, marked in its pending file; under a finish a tick says "Wants this task ticked off." with Tick it, and on its own every change names its task; the report is kept in this browser with the news for as long as a finish shows on the card.

(built as "Claude handed <task> back to you.", suggested in the chat, so it does not read like the error line); a round, "You should be able to view the round quickly from the bubble; it opens a small popup, and the same popup will open when we click Show The Answer ...   - **Reasoned while building:** "I don't want Claude to add to activity anymore" covers every line Claude's changes wrote, its add-tasks' "Added todo" and "New milestone" lines too, as the round's row listed them; a tick he gives from Claude's ask is his tick and keeps its Done line; Undo on a round takes its line back out; Quill's art ships as the city's character, doubled pixel for pixel to the portraits' 256 px, and a leader named Quill is him, so his face shows once; Quill says a round only when no finish carries it, since under a finish it is part of its leader's report; on a card, a line Quill says carries his small face, so the leader does not seem to say it; with the project view open, its leader card reads the news as the status card does, so news arriving then shows there; the leader card of a building with no leader has Proxima, as its status card does; the overdue line is dated the start of the day after its due date, once per due date, and draws no crowd; the popup is the Read panel's size.

- **Claude's Notes (27 October 2026, at his ask on Leader Bubbles, Round 2: "We need to talk about how Claude adds notes. What are they, how it decides to add notes or not, and what are they?" Built at 0.1.71; section 2, Claude's notes, and notes in a task.**
  - **K1, where each kind of note goes, kind by kind:** what a session did, Claude Activity, later (his idea of a record of what Claude did; nothing kept in the city until it is designed, the leader's line under the finish says it); where a long piece of work stands, in its task; a plan, in its task; a wizard round's answers in words, no note; a decision made with Claude, in its task; a correction or a fact found, no note; how something works or where it is, with his notes. NC while might keep some knowledge for agents, the main point is to be readable by humans."

Drawn in the city's pixel style with four more (map, signpost, checklist, warning) into `art/out/note-icons/`, for Round 2's I1.

- **Tasks (14 and 15 October 2026, Abdurrahman's ask for a brainstorm on the whole task system: "things to improve on tasks specifically, the entire task system, any missing features and things that might increase the quality of tasks and task tracking"). A two-round brainstorm, Tasks, Brainstorm 1; a ranking round, Tasks, Round 1; and the details, Tasks, Round 2 (41 questions), all delivered in the chat. Also kept in the claude.ai project as `claude/tasks-ideas.md`.**
  - **The goal (brainstorm):** "The speed of finishing many tasks".
  - **How they get built (15 October):** one idea at a time, in the order below; Abdurrahman tests each and decides what changes before the next one starts. Each idea's section of Round 2 holds its details; testing the one before can still change them. On 19 October he chose to build idea 4 before idea 3, which was not built yet; idea 3 comes after he tests idea 4. On 20 October he had idea 6 built ahead of ideas 3 and 5, then idea 5, still ahead of idea 3, then idea 7, designed and built the same day.
  - **The order (Round 1, A1):** 1. Tasks on the map (built at 0.1.39); 2. Recurring tasks and one-line adding; 3. Wizard round in a task; 4. Reports and the weekly review; 5. Tasks as cars; 6. Celebrations; 7. Archiving; 8. City-wide task list; 9. Attack System.
  - **Dropped:** project sleep (ranked third, then removed: an urgency of None does it). Killed in the brainstorm: a Done list, a week laid out by the city, task sizes, glowing paths between waiting tasks, a rule against priority levels, moving tasks by dropping them on buildings, a lost and found plaza, a date when a task stops mattering, a surprise task, the skill asking what done looks like, putting the effort into milestones instead, a task in several projects, the city logging unwritten work from Claude sessions, a Start button for a first step, checklist steps, the leader's kind word, the stale task sweep, folding done lists away, the drones flying back with one Tick it card, a cap on attacks, a combo streak, ticking several tasks at once, and a report Claude writes.
  - **1. Tasks on the map (M1 to M6), built at 0.1.39 (section 2):** a white count bubble with the next task in a line under it (M1); after 250 ms (M2); with No bubbles too, on demand (M3); pins from the task's panel, a project view row, a status card row, and What is next (M4); a pin clears only when ticked or unpinned (M5); each project's pin and one city star, separate, starring pinning too (M6). After trying it (18 October 2026), Abdurrahman asked for three changes, built at 0.1.40: What is next in a slightly different color, to read as its own widget, and the star in a new color (picked: neon cyan on night blue, used nowhere else); no star on the lists, only in the task's panel, since it is rare and was too much to see all the time; and the starred task in the side bar until it is unstarred or done, with its building, as a shortcut to the task. That 0.1.40 build misread one point: Abdurrahman meant What is next itself to change color while its task is starred, not the star a new color everywhere; Star and What is next, Round 1 (below) settled it, built at 0.1.41. Reasoned while building: the hover's count joins on the left so the bubbles there stay put; pins and the star live on the project, so Esc in the task panel does not undo them; the star bubble was purple with a gold star, so it never reads as yellow urgency; the hover leaves the name tip by the pointer as it was.

R3: by schedule (weekdays, monthly dates and years keep their rhythm; every day and every N days count from the tick). R4: one task moves on (only Activity's Done line records each tick). (it was shorthand for #weekly on the due date's weekday, so #weekly covers it). R1 was reasoned out at the build from his words, rather than asked again: ticking moves the task on and it goes away from every list and count until its next date, when it is back, due that day; it stays visible only in the project view, dimmed in its place with when it comes back, so it can be changed or stopped; a missed one stays one overdue task; Undo after a tick, Esc in the panel. ## 8. Working across chats and Claude Code

- **In claude.ai:** Nullovation City chats run in the Nullovation City project, which holds this file and the instructions. Each chat needs the newest source archive attached, since project knowledge takes documents but not zip archives, and a chat cannot write back to project files. The chat unzips it into `/home/claude`, which makes `/home/claude/nc`, works there, and delivers the built HTML, a new archive, and this file when it changed, to replace the project's copy.
- **In Claude Code:** Claude Code works in the git repo on the PC, or in an `nc` folder unzipped anywhere. Started there, it reads `CLAUDE.md`, which imports `docs/nullovation-city-knowledge.md`, and loads the two city skills from `.claude/skills/`; `d-wizard` comes from the personal skills folder. It runs natively on Windows, best with Git for Windows installed, or in WSL. On Windows, `python` replaces `python3` in every command.
- **Between them:** `python tools/pack.py` writes `nullovation-city-source-v<version>.zip` one folder up, beside the source folder, to attach to a chat. `python tools/pack.py --apply <zip>` brings the folder up to an archive a chat delivered: it writes changed files, removes files the archive no longer has, keeps what runs make and `.git/`, refuses an older archive unless `--force` is added, and shows the changes first with `--dry-run`.
- **Which copy wins:** `docs/nullovation-city-knowledge.md` in the newest archive is the current knowledge file. If the project's copy is older, the archive's copy wins.
- **What the archive leaves out:** `tests/shots/`, `tests/data/`, `art/out/`, `art/canon/out/`, `__pycache__/`, `.git/`, and Claude Code's local settings. It carries `index.html`, and keeps `art/unslimmed/` and `art/projects/`, which git leaves out.
- **GitHub:** the repo, github.com/AbdurrahmanKh/nullovation-city, is public and the home of the source. Since 0.1.73 the city's shared copy for sync lives in a private repository of its own, which the cities write themselves; Claude never touches it. Abdurrahman pushes; Claude never does. GitHub Pages serves `index.html` from the branch at https://abdurrahmankh.github.io/nullovation-city/, with `sw.js` and `src/app/` for the app since 0.1.67, so a push publishes the page and the app. `art/projects/` stays out of git. The public repo shows this file's project names; the tests use neutral names since 0.1.19.
